# Roadmap

Overall: M0 analysis tooling → M1 boot + kernel HLE → M2 title screen → M3 Hunter's Dream playable → M4 fully playable incl.
DLC → M5 enhancements (frame unlock, ultrawide, graphics). Below, M0 and M1 are detailed.
Every step has an **acceptance criterion** that can be checked with a command.

## M0 – Analysis tooling

| # | Step | Acceptance criterion | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| 0.1 | Repo, CMake, CI (Windows MSVC/Clang, Linux Clang), GPLv3 | CI green | ✅ local (MSVC) |
| 0.2 | `bbinstall validate/hash/install`: SFO, eboot format, data folder, DLC, SHA-256 manifest | `ctest` | ✅ |
| 0.3 | `bbelf`: segments, modules, libs, symbols/NIDs, relocations, CSV export | `ctest` | ✅ |
| 0.4 | Reference manifest from a verified 1.09 dump of our own (EU + US) → `manifests/` | `bbinstall validate --manifest` green; two independent sources (see `manifests/README.md`) | EU done (item 61): `manifests/CUSA03173-01.09.sha256`, checked against the content digests of base and update PKG; US open |
| 0.5 | NID name list: `tools/nidnames.py` fetches the shadPS4 NID table (GPL-2.0+, not copied into the repo) | `bbelf --names` resolves > 95 % of eboot imports | ✅ 701/701 |
| 0.6 | Ghidra headless script `tools/ghidra/import_bbelf.py`: loads ELF at link address, names GOT/PLT slots from `bbelf --csv` | Imports named in Ghidra | open |
| 0.7 | Signature matching of statically linked libraries: libc/libc++ (build OpenOrbis toolchain → FLIRT/Ghidra FID database), Havok (FID from public Havok SDK builds, if license-permitted, otherwise BinDiff against DS3 PC) | Measure share of named functions | open |
| 0.8 | Function matching against Dark Souls III PC (same engine generation) with BinDiff/Diaphora: candidates for frame timing, camera, param loader, file manager | List of ≥ 50 matched subsystem functions | open |
| 0.9 | Function finder for the recompiler: `.eh_frame` (PT_GNU_EH_FRAME → FDE list), then Zydis decoding of all known functions: direct `call`/`jmp` targets, RIP-relative `lea` (address taken, in PIE without relocation), `R_X86_64_RELATIVE` targets in the code range; ends of functions without FDE via recursive descent | Coverage ≥ 99 % of the code range, cross-checked with Ghidra | Coverage reached, Ghidra cross-check open: `bbelf --functions` finds 176,895 functions in the 1.00 eboot: 162,959 via `.eh_frame` (table and FDE `pc_begin` match in all cases, 0 overlaps), 13,927 via relocation (mainly vtable slots and chains of static initializers), 8 via `lea`, 1 via `call`. Code range `0xa0`–`0x2bbe9a5`: 44,436,803 bytes in functions + 1,432,514 bytes alignment padding = 100 %, no remaining gap (checked by script). 4 functions without FDE > 16 KB (max. 62 KB), fully reached via control flow. Open: indirect jumps end a path (jump tables → 0.10). Followed by PLT + rodata |
| 0.10 | Jump table detection (Clang PIC pattern `lea B,[rip+T]; movsxd R,[B+I*4]; add R,B; jmp R`, bound from `cmp I,imm` + `ja`/`jae`) and classification of all indirect `jmp` | All indirect `jmp` classified or marked as runtime fallback | done (Ghidra cross-check open): `bbelf --jumptables` on the 1.00 eboot classifies 26,863 of 26,863 indirect `jmp` (sum of classes is checked, deviation → exit 1): 3,919 switch tables (74,260 case targets, all within their function; 4 wrongly assigned `cmp` bounds discarded), 9,056 `jmp reg` tail calls (pointer loaded from memory or after epilogue), 11,565 `jmp [reg+disp]` tail calls, 624 `jmp [rip]`, 1,684 decoding artifacts from table data in the function body, 15 `jmp reg` unresolved (function pointers from arguments) → runtime fallback via the dispatch table |

Notes:
- `.eh_frame` is the best source for function boundaries. Clang also emits FDEs for C, and `PT_GNU_EH_FRAME` is
  present in the PS4 ELF.
- BinDiff against DS3 is worthwhile because FromSoftware engines share the same core (Dantelion generation; **assumption**,
  to be confirmed by matching).

## M1 – Recompiled code boots, kernel HLE, first logs

### 1.1 Recompiler core (`tools/bbrecomp`)
- Disassembler: [Zydis](https://github.com/zyantific/zydis) (MIT) instead of a custom one; covers AVX/F16C/BMI (Jaguar).
- IR-free direct translation per instruction to C++ (XenonRecomp pattern). Flags are currently **eager** (correct, simple);
  lazy flags via per-function liveness analysis are a later optimization, only after measurement.
- SIMD via `<immintrin.h>`, because the host is also x86-64 with AVX. This keeps the semantics bit-exact, without
  emulation.
- Output: N C++ files of ~5,000 functions each into `build/generated`, one dispatch table vaddr → function pointer.
- **Acceptance criterion:** Generated code for the synthetic test ELF plus a small OpenOrbis homebrew ELF (self-built,
  own license) compiles and produces the same output as under shadPS4.
- **Status:** `src/recomp/emitter.cpp` + `src/runtime/guest.h` translate the integer core (mov/lea/movzx/movsx(d),
  add/sub/cmp/and/or/xor/test/inc/dec/neg/not, shl/shr/sar, imul 2/3-operand, push/pop, call/ret, jmp/jcc, setcc, cmovcc,
  xchg, cdq/cdqe/cqo, pushfq/popfq, switch tables as `switch`). End-to-end test `recomp` (ctest): hand-assembled
  guest functions (loop, recursion, switch table, memory/partial registers, cmov/setcc, RFLAGS after
  add/sub/shl/sar/inc via `pushfq`) are translated at build time, compiled, executed and compared.
  Plus AVX-128 (vmov*, packed/scalar float ops, integer logic/add, shuffles/blends/dp/insert/cmp with imm,
  sqrt/rsqrt/rcp, conversions, (v)ucomiss/sd) via `<immintrin.h>` as well as `lock xadd`/`lock cmpxchg` via
  `std::atomic_ref`; checked in the `recomp` test with packed math, NaN comparison, shuffle and both atomics.
  Long tail added: adc/sbb, rol/ror, one-operand mul/imul/div/idiv (#DE → trap), bt/bts/btr/btc, BMI1
  bextr/andn, lzcnt/tzcnt/popcnt, movbe, rdtsc; AVX: 256-bit moves, vxorps ymm, vinsertf128/vextractf128, vpshufb,
  vpalignr, vpblendvb, vpcmpgtd, vpinsr/vpextr d/q, vpmin/maxsd, unpacks, byte shifts, vptest, vmovl/hps, double ops.
  **Native ground truth:** the same test additionally runs the fixture bytes directly on the host CPU (executable
  page, Win64→SysV trampoline) and compares RAX, RFLAGS (only architecturally defined flags) and memory with the
  recompiled run – 9,600 runs with random and boundary values, all identical.
  On the 1.00 eboot (`bbrecomp --coverage`): 10,610,094 reachable instructions, **99.98 %** translated (89.99 % integer only,
  99.38 % with AVX-128, 99.88 % after the first tail). Remainder 2,159: deliberate traps (`ud2`, `int3`, `cli`/`sti`, `in`/
  `out`, `int`, `hlt`, `iretd`, `syscall`) and 1 `or` with GS segment (GS base not modelled). Recently added:
  x87 (doubles instead of 80 bit), MMX, string instructions with `rep`, `leave`/`enter`, `rcl`/`rcr`, `shld`/`shrd`, `bsf`/`bsr`,
  blsi/blsr/blsmsk, ymm forms and the Round/Cvt/Pack/Unpack/Blend families; agent fixtures run natively against the CPU
  (24,900 comparison runs). Limits: no 80-bit x87, `cpuid` fixed Jaguar value, AF not modelled.
  Note: `rsqrtps/rcpps` are approximations; AMD (Jaguar) and Intel hosts may differ in the last bit.
- **Verification against real eboot code (`bbdiff`, local, `cmake -DBB_EBOOT=<eboot.bin>`):** guest memory
  identity-mapped at `0x400000` (guest address = host address), image loaded, 6,000 evenly distributed
  leaf functions (of 65,671: fully translated, without calls/`fs:`/indirect jumps except tables) are each run once
  natively and once recompiled, with random registers and pointers into a terminating scratch structure (MASM trampoline
  with full register set, faults caught by a vectored exception handler, hangs by a watchdog).
  Compared: all 16 GPRs incl. `rsp`, xmm0–15, scratch/stack and all writable segments. Result (1.00 eboot,
  3 inputs per function): **14,792 of 14,792 runs identical**, 3,208 skipped (native access faults due to
  random pointers). Mutation test (`f_add`→`f_sub` at 153 sites in the generated code) is detected. Flags at return
  are not compared (often architecturally undefined). The harness gives the translated version the same
  return address as the native one (otherwise loops ending via stack words end at different places). Only
  leaf functions (no calls, `fs:`, indirect jumps except tables) are checked. Linux/GAS trampoline open.

### 1.2 Runtime (`src/runtime`)
- Guest address space: reservation at link address (`VirtualAlloc`/`mmap MAP_FIXED_NOREPLACE`), load segments,
  apply `RELATIVE` relocs, zero `.bss`.
- TLS: copy the `PT_TLS` template per thread. `fs_base` in the `Context` points to the TCB; the kernel HLE supplies
  `DTPMOD/TPOFF` values.
- Init order: `DT_INIT`/`DT_INIT_ARRAY`, then `_start`/entry with the PS4 argument block (`argc`, `argv`, env,
  auxv equivalent per shadPS4 `linker.cpp` as reference).
- **Acceptance criterion:** `_start` runs up to the first HLE call; log shows NID and name.
- **Status:** `src/runtime/loader.cpp` places `PT_LOAD` segments at vaddr + load bias, zeroes `.bss` and applies
  `RELATIVE` relocs (value = addend + bias). The bias is set at recompile time (`kLoadBias` in the generated
  code, default `0x400000` for images linked at 0) and applied throughout: RIP-relative addresses,
  return addresses, switch case values, dispatch table. Checked by `recomp_default` and `recomp_bias`
  (same test, bias `0x100000`). Identity mapping, TLS, init order and entry are implemented (see below).
- **Full build (local, `-DBB_EBOOT=<eboot.bin>`, target `bbgame`):** All 89 shards (2000 functions each, approx. 757 MiB C++)
  compile without errors and link into an approx. 1.1 GiB `bbgame.exe` (176,896 functions, MSVC,
  `/arch:AVX /bigobj /Od`, 16 threads: approx. 205 s). `/O2` is approx. 4× slower per shard (estimated approx. 14 min), objects
  30–60 % smaller. The emitter now generates `goto`/`case` only to decoded instruction starts (previously approx. 190
  `C2094` errors in 40 shards caused by jump table entries behind the bounds check); everything else becomes a trap.
- **First start (`bbgame <eboot.bin>`):** guest memory identity-mapped at `0x400000`: image, cells for 20
  data imports (`STT_OBJECT`: `__stack_chk_guard` with fixed canary, `_Stdout`, vtables, typeinfo), TLS block, heap
  64 MiB, stack 8 MiB. `setup_main_thread` (TLS/TCB, entry stack, `rdi` = argument block, `rsi` = exit function) and
  `run_entry` (DT_INIT, init array, entry; entry/DT_INIT/DT_FINI are now starting points of the function search, because
  `DT_INIT` lies before the first unwind entry). Result: run up to `bbrt: unimplemented import libc 3GPpjQdAMTw
  __cxa_guard_acquire` – acceptance criterion "log with NID and name at the first HLE call" met. TLS on the real eboot: PT_TLS
  `memsz 0x750`, `filesz 0`, 17,122 `fs:` accesses are all `mov reg, fs:[0]`, no TPOFF/DTPMOD relocations.
  Open: HLE (M1.3), calling-convention details at start (whether the start code reads `[rsp]` instead of `rdi`), 1.09 dump.

### 1.3 HLE framework (`src/hle`)
- Registration: `{nid, library, fn}` tables per library. Unimplemented imports become a stub that
  logs NID/name/caller and, depending on the setting, aborts or returns 0.
- **libkernel** (order by call frequency during boot, determined via shadPS4 trace):
  `sceKernelGetProcParam`, `__error`, `sceKernelUsleep`, `sceKernelGetProcessTime*`, `scePthread*` (→ `std::thread`
  + own TLS context), Mutex/Cond/RWLock, `sceKernelCreateEqueue/WaitEqueue`, event flags, semaphores,
  `sceKernelAllocateDirectMemory/MapDirectMemory/MapFlexibleMemory`, `sceKernelOpen/Read/Lseek/Stat` (+ `/app0` →
  `game/`, `mods/` takes precedence), `sceKernelLoadStartModule` (for `sce_module/*.prx` → HLE replacement).
- libc: comes as `libc.prx` from `sce_module` (confirmed). The 216 libc imports go to host libc wrappers
  (respect SysV structures such as `FILE`, `errno`, `locale`).
- Fios2: 17 imports (confirmed), `libSceFios2.prx` likewise lies in `sce_module`. File access goes through Fios2 and must
  be mapped to the VFS (`/app0` → `game/`) in M1.
- **Acceptance criterion:** boot up to the first Gnm/VideoOut use without a crash; all imports called up to then
  implemented, log complete.

**Status M1.3 (on the 1.00 eboot):** `src/hle/{hle,libc,libc_wrap,kernel,thread,vfs,system,net,np,audio,crash,guest_heap}.cpp`.
Startup (`_start`, not DT_INIT/init array separately – that constructed all statics twice) now runs through the
static constructors, creates worker threads, boots heaps (5 GiB direct-memory counter), reads `/app0/dvdroot_ps4/sound/*`
(FMOD banks), reports `sceNetSocket Failed` (offline, intended) and proceeds into GX initialization ("DefRenderTarget",
thread `GXRenderThread`). The render thread checks via `sceKernelQueryMemoryProtection` whether its 8 MiB block is mapped GPU-readable
(`prot & 0x10`) – that is why `note_mapping` records the protection mask requested by the guest. Afterwards it calls
**Gnm functions for the first time** (`sceGnmGetTheTessellationFactorRingBufferBaseAddress`, `sceGnmValidateCommandBuffers`,
`sceGnmMapComputeQueue`, …). This is the **first Gnm use; the M1 acceptance criterion remains open** (it requires "without a crash";
`_ZNSt8ios_base4InitC1Ev`/`_ZNSt6_WinitC1Ev` are still stubs; at the end an access fault at 0xdc10).
Causes of the following crashes (all fixed, each found via `BB_TRACE_FN`/`BB_DUMP`/`bad_access`): `sceGnmMapComputeQueue`
must return a queue ID ≥ 1 (0 = error, the engine tears down the device); `sceGnmDrawInitDefaultHardwareState200` must return the
dwords actually written, not the buffer size (otherwise the command buffer counts as full).
Status: GX device, VideoOut/Equeue, UserService (synthetic user 1), Pad (neutral, connected), PlayGo, Rtc (UTC only),
C stdio via the VFS, Gnm submits (stub), Trophy/SaveData init (stub) and `operator new` run. The engine runs in its
**main loop (approx. 31 iterations/s; this is the callback rate, not the display frame rate**; measured in the `/O2` build: 2833 `sceNetCtlCheckCallback` calls in 90 s; the
frame limiter waits via a `gettimeofday` busy-wait), parses the PARAM tables and loads menu assets
(`/app0/dvdroot_ps4/menu/*.tpf.dcx`, `*.gfx`; ~650 `sceKernelRead`/577 `fread` in 90 s). The engine **already submits command buffers**
(90 s run: 103 `sceGnmSubmitCommandBuffers`, 21 `sceGnmSubmitAndFlipCommandBuffers`, 43 `DingDong`; `sceGnmIsUserPaEnabled` returns 0; a test with 1 changed
nothing in the submits, stays 0). Measured PM4 mix (`BB_PM4_STATS=1`, 50 s run, 490 buffers / 170,865 dwords; scanner now error-free: zero dwords are
buffer padding, not type-0 packets – a type-0 header with value 0 would have swallowed the next header): `SET_CONTEXT_REG` (0x69) 12221,
`NOP` (0x10) 3095, `SET_SH_REG` (0x76) 2382, `EVENT_WRITE_EOP` (0x47) 1543, `ACQUIRE_MEM` (0x58) 1122, `WAIT_REG_MEM` (0x3c) 884,
`DISPATCH_DIRECT` (0x15) 565, `SET_UCONFIG_REG` (0x79) 330, `NUM_INSTANCES` (0x2f) 254, `DRAW_INDEX_AUTO` (0x2d) 239, `DMA_DATA` (0x50) 188,
`EVENT_WRITE` (0x46) 160, `INDEX_TYPE` (0x2a) 91, `INDEX_BASE` (0x26) 15, `DRAW_INDEX_OFFSET_2` (0x35) 15. Opcode names checked against Mesa
`src/amd/common/sid.h` (0x15 = DISPATCH_DIRECT, 0x16 = DISPATCH_INDIRECT).
Scanner validation criterion: `bad=0` and `overrun=0` (no packet runs past the buffer end; 62,868 dwords, of which 11,466 zeros
padding). `type0=0` is an observation on the buffers of *this* title, not a guarantee (SET_*_REG are type-3 packets; an interpreter
must treat zeros as padding only at packet boundaries). First effect of the submits: `EVENT_WRITE_EOP`/`WRITE_DATA`/`INDIRECT_BUFFER`
are executed (fence memory is written; flip count in the 90 s run 16–27 across different runs, i.e. noise – no confirmed effect; A/B without fence code and checking the `sceVideoOutGetFlipStatus` fields `gcQueueNum`/`flipPendingNum` are still outstanding). **Nothing** is rendered yet (M2). M1 acceptance criterion met except for the two ios init stubs.
- Builds: `/Od` + `BB_TRACE_CALLS` (`build_full`) is approx. 4× slower and for diagnostics only; the `/O2` build
  (`-DBB_GAME_OPT=O2 -DBB_TRACE_CALLS=OFF`, `build_o2`, ~50 min compile, `bbgame.exe` 270 MB) is the reference build.
  Timing values from the `/Od` build are not engine performance.
- Profiler: `BB_PROFILE_SECS=<s>` samples the main thread (host RIP → `bbgame.map`); `BB_HANG_SECS` additionally prints the
  most frequent HLE imports (`HLE call counts`).
- Build cost: every CMake change in the `BB_EBOOT` block regenerates/rebuilds all shards (`/O2` ≈ 25–50 min); SDL3/volk/Vulkan headers are fetched via FetchContent only there; the default build and `ctest` remain independent of it.
- Profile (`/O2`, `BB_PROFILE_SECS=40`, all threads): main thread 100 % in the main loop (`f_21b9830`, frame-limiter busy-wait),
  all loader/pool threads (profiler key is now name + OS thread ID; several threads share names such as `SpClothVertexUpdate`/`HavokWorkerThread`, so their totals say nothing about individual threads) (`Core.Res.FileLoader`, `PostProcessor_*`, `EzWorkPool_*`, …) block in semaphore/cond waits – loading
  runs in step with the main loop (~4 iterations per file), not CPU-bound. Whether this matches the original is open.
- **Presentation (start of M2):** `src/gpu/present.{h,cpp}` – SDL3 window (main thread, `bb::gpu::run`) + Vulkan swapchain (volk,
  FIFO/vsync); every guest flip (`sceVideoOutSubmitFlip`/`sceGnmSubmitAndFlipCommandBuffers`) calls through `hle::set_flip_hook`
  `request_present`. Until real rendering exists, the swapchain frame is cleared to a color depending on the flip counter
  (heartbeat). Verified (RTX 3070 Ti, Windows): window "BloodbornePC" 1280×720 shows the clear. This confirms instance/device/swapchain and the flip→present path; the flips themselves come from the still-unrendered guest submits, so no game image appears. Flip count `sceGnmSubmitAndFlipCommandBuffers` per 90 s (`/O2`): headless 75 and 46, with window 51 (earlier runs 16–27, one 50 s run 113):
the spread between runs (loading state/threading) is larger than any difference caused by the presenter; there is no confirmed backlog;
main loop always ~31 iterations/s. Closing the window terminates the process hard (`_Exit`), no orderly guest shutdown. `BB_HEADLESS=1` disables the window.
  Dependencies: SDL3 (zlib), volk (MIT), Vulkan headers (Apache-2.0) via FetchContent, only in the local `BB_EBOOT` build.
  `gpu_clock()` for EOP timestamps is monotonic (ns base, ~1 tick/10 ns, not the 1-tick/ns of the real GPU).
- Display buffers (`BB_FLIP_STATS=1`): `sceVideoOutRegisterBuffers` registers 3 buffers 1920×1080, pitch 1920, tiling 0, format 0x80000000
  (A8R8G8B8_SRGB); the flips cycle 0, 1, 2 (triple buffering, `flipArg` counts up, mode 1), all buffers still entirely 0 (no
  GPU execution). Lesson: `int` arguments (buffer index) have garbage in the upper register bits – always mask to 32 bits. (`PM4: 0 buffers` in an
  earlier run was only because `BB_PM4_STATS` was unset, not a hook error.)
- Register shadow + draw log (`BB_DRAW_LOG=1`): `sceGnmSetPsShader/SetVsShader/SetCsShader` now write their fixed packet sequences
  (0x28/0x1d/0x19 dwords incl. trailing NOP; registers checked against shadPS4 `gnmdriver.cpp`, prototype `(cmdbuf, size, regs[, modifier])`).
  Consequence: the PM4 no longer contains zero dwords, and the first draw (`DRAW_INDEX_AUTO`, 3 indices = full-screen triangle) shows `CB_COLOR0_BASE` =
  0x1051418000 (= display buffer 0, INFO 0x28828), `ps=0x10d5b10f00`, `vs=0x10d5b10d00`. The shader binaries are real GCN code
  (start `beeb03ff 0000000x` = s_mov_b32 literal header, then SOP/VOP1/VOP2, export `f80008cf`): **input for the GCN→SPIR-V translator
  is therefore directly readable.** Still stubs: `sceGnmDrawInitDefaultHardwareState200` (needs real HW default registers) and the remaining `sceGnm*`.
  `sceKernelGetdirentries` layout confirmed against shadPS4 `OrbisKernelDirent` (8-byte header; ino64 variant does not apply; `nbytes` is `u64`; `basep` is output = offset *before* the read, as in shadPS4 `NormalDirectory::getdents`). Deviation: real Orbis packs dirents into 512-byte blocks (last `reclen` per block reaches to the block end, `nbytes` is rounded down to 512); we deliver densely packed – a guest that only follows `reclen` is therefore safe, a guest with fixed 512-byte blocks is untested. Mnemonics are no longer guessed since the decoder (`src/gpu/gcn.{h,cpp}`, opcode tables from Mesa ACO via `tools/gen_gcn_ops.py`); the source of the mapping is the generated `gcn_ops.inc` (Mesa `aco_opcodes.py`, MIT).
- Measurable M2 criterion: `BB_FLIP_STATS` reports words ≠ 0 for a display buffer for the first time (output "nonzero words", today 0/1,048,576; raw 32-bit words of the – at tiling 0 swizzled – bytes are counted, so tiling-independent). Note: tiling mode 0 is at
  `sceVideoOut` *TILE* (1 = LINEAR); an image dump of the buffers therefore needs de-tiling (8×8 micro tiles), not a simple PPM.
- **M2 – GCN→SPIR-V and Vulkan backend (status, everything measured against the local game):**
  - `src/gpu/gcn.{h,cpp}`: GFX7 decoder/disassembler (all formats); `BB_SHADER_DIR=<dir>` writes each distinct shader as `.bin/.txt/.spv`
    (length exactly from the `OrbShdr` trailer, `ShaderInfo`). `src/gpu/gcn_spirv.{h,cpp}` translates VS/PS/CS (one SPIR-V invocation = one GCN lane;
    exec/VCC as lane bools; SGPR values from user data + memory are tracked symbolically, so descriptor chains are re-evaluated per draw
    without re-translating: `eval_resources`, `shape_mask`; fetch shader inlined via `s_swappc_b64`). Supported: SALU subset, VALU float/int/cmp,
    interpolation, exports, `buffer_load/store(_format)` (8/16/32-bit channels, dst_sel), `image_load/_mip/store/sample(_l/_lz/_b)`, forward jumps only
    over exec-predicated blocks. Not supported (reports an error instead of translating incorrectly): real loops/SCC branches, LDS/DS, MTBUF, tessellation/GS, flat attributes (`v_interp_mov`), 64-bit/F16 ALU.
  - Test: translate all 8 shaders occurring at boot (4 CS, 2 PS, 2 VS) and **validate against Vulkan 1.2 (SPIRV-Tools)**; `ctest` has `gcn` (decoder) and `spirv` (hand-assembled shaders + validator; `bbspv` validates files).
  - `src/gpu/backend.{h,cpp}` + `vk_context.{h,cpp}` (VMA): PM4 draws/dispatches run via `gpu::hooks()` (`gpu_hooks.h`) in Vulkan 1.3 (dynamic rendering): render targets from `CB_COLOR*`
    (address → VkImage), pipelines from context registers (blend, cull, topology; RECTLIST via geometry shader), descriptors from V#/T#/S# (buffers are copied per draw into a ring and written back after
    CS writes), textures: linear and 1D-thin de-tiled, RT aliasing; **2D macro tiling (index 14 among others), mips, depth and MSAA are missing** (texture stays empty, log).
    The presenter blits the flipped display-buffer RT. In the 60 s run: ~200 draws/475 dispatches executed (of which 133 skipped: draws without a color target or without PS = depth-only passes), crash-free; **only black/empty is visible**, because the source texture of the first post pass (display buffer 3) is empty – there is still no game image.
  - No Vulkan validation layer installed: correctness of the backend calls is only evidenced by driver acceptance and crash-freedom, not by validation.
- **SaveData (`src/hle/savedata.cpp`):** real directories `<BB_SAVE_DIR|./savedata>/<user>/<TITLE_ID>/<dir>` mounted as `/savedataN` (Mount/Umount/GetMountInfo/SetParam/SaveIcon/DirNameSearch/Delete, save data memory as a file, dialog stubs). On first start the game mounts `SPRJ0005` with `RDONLY` → `NOT_FOUND` (as on a fresh console); how it continues after that is open.
- **Boot stall resolved (recompiler bug, not an HLE bug):** The emitter generated `xchg [mem],reg` and all `lock` RMW ALU instructions (`add/sub/and/or/xor/inc/dec/neg/not/adc/sbb`) on memory non-atomically (read then write). The engine's spin locks (`f_21b9830`, main thread 100 % in the wait loop, all workers asleep) were thereby lost by chance. Now: `xchg` with memory operand = `atom<T>().exchange`, `lock` RMW = compare-exchange loop (`src/recomp/emitter.cpp`). Measurement (`/O2`, headless, `BB_NO_GPU=1`, 25 s each): before, 4 of 8 runs reached `sceSaveDataInitialize` (earlier series 2 of 4); afterwards **8 of 8, each time with identical timestamps (Initialize 2 s, Mount 4 s)**, i.e. deterministic up to this point. With the GPU backend and `BB_GPU_LOG` the main loop then keeps running steadily (~100 draws/s, 1809 draws in 22 s), the same with an existing and with a fresh (→`NOT_FOUND`) save directory. Still non-atomic: `lock bts/btr/btc` on memory. Discarded hypotheses: `basep`, GPU backend, SaveData/services, mixing of time sources, Cond/Mutex (FreeBSD semantics for `cond_wait` nevertheless implemented).
- **Draw tally (`BB_GPU_LOG`, 22 s):** 1809 draws, 4254 dispatches, 1205 draws skipped: 603 without color target (`CB_TARGET_MASK`=0) and 602 without pixel shader (`shader`, PS address 0) – per register state depth-only passes (assumption, not checked individually). All rendered draws go into the three display-buffer RTs. `BB_GPU_STATS=1` reads the RTs back: **all three 0 of 9,437,184 bytes ≠ 0** (black), because the source texture of the post pass (tiling index 14) stays empty.
- **M2 reached – the title screen appears in the window** (screenshot of the `BloodbornePC` window, RTX 3070 Ti, 40 s after start: Bloodborne logo, background image, "Play Online / Play Offline", copyright line). The chain leading there, each confirmed by a render-target dump (`BB_GPU_DUMP=<dir>`, `BB_GPU_STATS=1`, `BB_GPU_DUMP_AT=a-b`):
  1. `DRAW_INDEX_OFFSET_2` (0x35) + `INDEX_BASE` (0x26) and `DMA_DATA` (0x50) were missing from the PM4 interpreter: ~7400 indexed draws (the entire UI) were ignored, only the 3 post passes ran.
  2. `tbuffer_*` (MTBUF) with format taken from the instruction; missing VOP2 opcodes (`v_bfm_b32`, `v_ldexp_f32`, `v_cvt_pknorm_*`).
  3. Compute writes the tonemap LUT via `image_store` to the same memory location that the post pass samples: sampled and storage use now share one VkImage; T# word 5 (`base_array`/`last_array`) selects the array layer per descriptor (the three dispatches each write one layer, which is why previously only one color channel was there).
  4. Decoder: `v_madmk_f32`/`v_madak_f32` always carry the constant K *always* in the following dword; without that the rest of the shader shifted by one dword (background tiled 2×2 and mirrored, because the UV/position matrix was computed wrongly). Test in `test_gcn`.
  All 24 shaders of the boot/title sequence translate, no error in the log. Open/known: aspect ratio is not preserved when blitting to the window; 2D macro tiling (index 14), mips, depth, MSAA are still missing (not needed for the title screen; the post-pass source RT is aliased by address).
- Next step: input (pad) so the title menu becomes operable, then game start → Hunter's Dream: 2D macro tiling (index 14 among others), mips, depth attachment, `sceGnmDrawInitDefaultHardwareState200`, further shader features according to the corpus (`BB_SHADER_DIR`).
- **Jump tables (recompiler):** `bbelf --jumptables` determined the size of many tables incorrectly (three hard aborts `switch: target outside table` at 0x14cd67a, 0xdb0a0e, 0x2e6e75): the bounds check `cmp idx,N; jbe <table code>` (instead of `ja <default>`) was not recognized, and a second, independent range check on the same register could displace the correct one. Now: all comparisons in the window are candidates (only those that trace back to the index – directly or via `mov`; `jb/jbe` only if the target lies *before* the `jmp`), result = the larger of "best candidate" and "previous heuristic / reading up to the first entry outside the function". Too large only costs unused `case` lines; too small ends the game. Effect: 81,668 instead of 74,260 case targets, no table has fewer entries than before (116 larger). Run: ≥ 120 s without `unsupported` abort.
- **Ajm (`src/hle/ajm.cpp`):** batch jobs (`sceAjmBatchJob{Control,Run,RunSplit}BufferRa`, `InlineBuffer`, `StartBuffer`, `Wait`) are tracked in a page table by batch address (the guest stores jobs with the SDK sizes 0x40/0x50; a custom record format in the guest buffer crashed) and "executed" synchronously: decoding is **not** implemented (silence, entire input consumed); sideband output in SDK order (Result, Stream, Format, Gapless, MFrame, Codec-Info) incl. ATRAC9 codec info from the config data (frame samples/superframe as in LibAtrac9). `sceSystemServiceGetStatus`/`ReceiveEvent` added. Without these stubs the game hung/crashed at t≈23 s (`sprj_pscom.fsb`).
- **Game progress now (O2, headless, with GPU backend):** deterministic boot sequence: Initialize 2 s, SaveData mount 4 s, logos `logo_sce`/`logo_jstudio`/`logo_fromsoft` at 9/14/18 s (≈ 4–5 s per logo, as on the original), at 23 s sound bank + Ajm, after that no further file accesses; main loop ~30 Hz (frame-limiter busy-wait on `gettimeofday`), ~89 draws/s, no crash in 120 s. The game is at the title screen and waits for input (now visible, see M2 below).
- **Input + menu navigation (`src/gpu/pad.{h,cpp}`, SDL3 gamepad + keyboard; checked via key press into the window):** Arrows=D-pad, Enter/Space=Cross, Backspace/Esc=Circle, WASD/IJKL=sticks. The game **confirms with Circle** (the in-game legend shows "Circle: OK"; neither `sceSystemServiceParamGetInt(1000)` nor `sceAppContentAppParamGetInt` (now returns `USER_DEFINED_PARAM_n` from `param.sfo`, EU: 13) affects this; the source of the mapping is unexplained). Key presses stay visible until the next `scePadReadState` (a 60-ms timer was lost at ~1 fps). Path reached (reproducible, ~45 s startup): title → "Play Offline" → offline dialog → brightness → Controls → **character creation**: name via `sceImeDialog*` (`src/hle/ime.cpp`: no keyboard UI, closes after 10 polls with `BB_IME_TEXT` or "Hunter"), dialog "Finalize contract?" appears.
- **Renderer findings in character creation (each evidenced by draw dump + register log):**
  - The "distorted icon grid" was **stencil clipping**: the game draws the whole 1024×128 BC1 icon strip and masks it via `DB_DEPTH_CONTROL` (stencil, LEQUAL, ref 1). Depth/stencil is now implemented (`get_ds`, DB_* registers, pipeline state, dynamic stencil values, depth-only draws without PS, sampling of depth). Afterwards the stat list is correct and the 3D character preview appears as a silhouette.
  - Gnm helper compute shaders (fill/copy, identified by code hash) wrote render/depth buffers as *buffers*; here they live in VkImages. They now run as image operations (`vkCmdClearColorImage`, depth/stencil clear, image copy), or as CPU fill/copy for ordinary memory.
  - Performance: buffer V# were copied up to 64 MB per draw (65 GB in 2 min, ~2 frames/s). Now: vertex buffers only up to the highest index, large aligned buffers via `VK_EXT_external_memory_host` without copy, typed-buffer stores without read-modify-write. Measurement (RTX 3070 Ti, character creation, `BB_GPU_LOG`): ~2200 recorded draws/s (previously 88–200); one frame there has ≈ 76 draws. Profiler: `BB_GPU_PROF=1` (timestamp queries per pipeline), `BB_CS_LOG`, `BB_SKIP_CS`.
  - Open: background remnants (old text under new, "Entry Incomplete" → "Hunter") – probably unrecognized clears of HDR RTs (R11G11B10); `buffer_*_format` with dfmt 6/15, MSAA images (type 14/15), cube arrays, 2D macro tiling (index 14).
  - `setjmp`/`longjmp`: the emitter generates inline code for calls to `setjmp` (registers + resume address into the guest `jmp_buf`); `longjmp` throws `bb::rt::LongJmp`, the calling function catches it and continues after the `setjmp`. Trigger: the first `longjmp` after "Finalize contract → Yes" ended in `ud2` (0xa3217a). Not verified: whether the path has been reached since (the run crashes earlier, see below).
  - Further changes in this step: `lock bts/btr/btc` on memory as CAS loop; `modf/modff`, `_Dtest/_FDtest`; `v_mad_legacy/mac_legacy/rcp_iflag/rsq_clamp/mul_hi`, `v_cube*` + cube sampling, forward `s_cbranch_scc0/1` (exec predication), `s_load` with 32-bit literal offset; T# pitch for linear textures; scissor clipping (viewport/generic/window/screen, `VPORT_SCISSOR_ENABLE`); `/app0/sce_sys` from `Sc0/` under PKG extractor layout (vfs: longest prefix wins); `free()` on foreign pointers is ignored and logged in the HLE heap; Mutex/Cond/RwLock with sentinel handles (0/1 = static initializer, 2/3 = destroyed → `EINVAL` instead of crash).
  - **M3 blocker resolved (cause: unhandled command-buffer space):** The "pool/freelist corruption" after "Finalize contract → Yes" was a secondary error. The engine reserves fixed command-buffer ranges for `sceGnmSetLs/Hs/Es/GsShader` (23 dwords) and the marker functions (`sceGnmInsertPush/Pop/Set(Color)Marker`: `(cmdbuf, dwords, …)`) and advances the write pointer by that size; the imports were stubs (or did nothing), and the range contained stale data from earlier frames. The PM4 interpreter executed these as packets (among others `WRITE_DATA`/`EVENT_WRITE_EOP` with garbage addresses such as `0x2f5c0026900`) and thereby overwrote guest heap (freelist pointer `2`, shifting crash sites). Now: `fill_reserved_nop` fills the reserved range with a NOP (LS/HS/ES/GS without translation → their draws are still skipped); `mem_ok()` checks all PM4 target addresses (`WRITE_DATA`, `EVENT_WRITE_EOP`, `RELEASE_MEM`, `DMA_DATA`, `INDIRECT_BUFFER`) against the guest address space and logs outliers with their context. Additionally: compute queues (`sceGnmMapComputeQueue` records ring/read pointer, `sceGnmDingDong` executes the new ring section synchronously and sets the read pointer; previously the engine waited in a spin loop on "Stall during rendering at flush N"), `RELEASE_MEM` (0x49), `qsort` (guest comparator via `call_indirect`, stable), mutex destroy under `g_create_mutex` returns EBUSY while the mutex is held, mount table (`vfs.cpp`) under mutex, anonymous maps on a 2 MiB grid. Verified (O2, pad script): 200 s without crash, 3400 flips, the game loads `m24_01_00_00` (Hunter's Dream), `c0000`, objects, sound banks. **The window stays black** (only a grey bar at the bottom): shader translations are missing (`image_sample` with image type 15, `buffer_load_format` dfmt 15/6/0, `v_addc_co_u32`, SOPK, unusual control flow), texture format dfmt=6/nfmt=7 (type 9), tiling index 14/19/0 – this is the next work list.
    - **Status at end of session (for the next session):**
    - New: `ds_swizzle_b32` (quad permute/bit mask within the quad, PS only, via `GroupNonUniformQuad`; quad lane from `FragCoord`); null descriptors (T# or V# entirely `0xFFFFFFFF` = unbound) read 0 / stores are dropped (resolved ~35 "not translated" cases); shader length is limited to the window that was read; `mem_ok` log per opcode; `thread_create` publishes the handle **before** thread start (libthr behavior); debug `BB_TRACE_COND=1` (waiter table + signal counter, printed by the hang watchdog); crash report names the module of the fault RIP.
    - Measurement (O2, pad script, ~190 s): no crash, PM4 `bad=0 overrun=0`, ~1400 frames, window shows the **loading screen** ("Good. All signed and sealed." + full bar), then no further file accesses (last file `sound/sprj_sm24_eng.fsb`). Hunter's Dream itself is **still not visible**. The main thread waits in frame sync (`f_219a0f0`, condvar, 0 signals) for the GXRenderThread, which is actively building draw lists – whether this is only slow (≈10 frames/s) or a real hang is **unexplained**.
    - Open observation: a run with `BB_TRACE_COND` set (old variant with 10 s `wait_for` loop) reproducibly aborted with `indirect transfer to unknown guest address 0x57b3240` (data vaddr 0x53b3240, caller in a destructor, `jmp rax` via broken vtable); not seen again with the current registry variant → probably a latent race in guest/HLE, time-dependent. Not investigated.
    - Next steps: (1) find out why the loading screen does not end (measure frame rate with `BB_FLIP_STATS`; render-thread profile `BB_PROFILE_SECS`; is the game waiting on FMOD/audio – `sceAudioOutOutput` is a stub pacer, no ATRAC9/Ajm decode?); (2) remaining shaders: `v_addc_co_u32`, `v_writelane_b32`, `v_lshl_b64`, SOPK, control flow, `image_sample_d`, `buffer_load_format` dfmt 0/6, `buffer_atomic_add`, LDS (`ds_read/write`, compute), texture format dfmt=6/nfmt=7, tiling index 14/19/0; (3) reduce draw count per frame (CPU-side PM4/backend overhead); (4) then audio (ATRAC9), FPS/resolution decoupling, Linux build, 1.09/DLC.
    - Review notes that were checked and rejected: RELEASE_MEM field order (header does not count in `b[]`; layout matches PACKET3_RELEASE_MEM), "count field 0x323" (correct 0x32), qsort stack "256 bytes below rsp" (space below the call return address is free), address arithmetic 0x57b3240−0x400000=0x173b240 (correct 0x53b3240, data).
    - **Open/unverified:** `RELEASE_MEM` (0x49) is implemented per AMD layout (event b[0], control b[1], address b[2]/b[3], data b[4]/b[5]), but was not executed in any run – check it first if compute labels show anomalies (one review claimed the order is shifted by 1, not evidenced). `mem_ok()` uses a fixed window [0x400000, 0xF000000000) instead of `gpu::hooks().mem_valid`; `submit_scan` does not check the top-level pointer.
    - Working method: build `build_o2` via `build/o2b.cmd` (`-j 3`); `build_full`/trace build deleted (rebuild as needed with `-DBB_GAME_OPT=Od`, `BB_TRACE_CALLS`, ~6 min). Runs: `bbgame.exe <eboot>` with `BB_STUB_IMPORTS=1`, `BB_SAVE_DIR=build\savedata_test`, `BB_PAD_SCRIPT` (see above); then terminate the process.
  - **Debug tools (local, trace build `build_full` with `BB_TRACE_CALLS`)**: `BB_WATCH=<hex>` (stores to address), `BB_WATCH_VALUE=<hex>` (every 8-byte store of this value, with the last 12 function entries), `BB_TRACE_HLE=1` + `BB_WATCH` (reports value change *inside* an HLE call), `BB_MAP_LOG=1` (direct memory allocs/maps), `BB_GPU_PROF=1` (GPU time per pipeline), `BB_CS_LOG`, `BB_SKIP_CS`, `BB_ONE_CORE`.
  - **End of loading screen clarified (follow-up session, O2, pad script, RTX 3070 Ti):** The statement "no file accesses afterwards" was wrong – the game keeps loading (`BB_TRACE_IO` now has timestamps). The cause of the slowness was **not** audio/compute but the GPU backend:
    1. `write_descriptors` copied, for buffer V# on unmapped/null addresses (V# with base < 0x10000, large `num_records`), up to 64 MB per draw via `memset` into the upload ring (≈ 1 ms/draw, 74–83 % of wall time in the backend, main loop ≈ 4 fps; without GPU backend `BB_NO_GPU=1` ≈ 28 fps). Now the head of the ring (64 MB, `kZeroBytes`) stays permanently zero and is bound for read-only null/unmapped buffers (`zero binds`). Measurement before/after (window 80–90 s): backend draw time/draw from ≈ 1.1 ms to ≈ 0.09 ms; the loading phase `sprj_sm24_eng.fsb` now ends at ≈ 95–125 s instead of ≈ 215 s.
    2. Follow-on crash at high draw rate (`0xc0000005`, access 0x130 in `nvoglv64.dll`, caller `Backend::draw`): `ring_alloc` (index copy) flushes on ring wrap *inside* `vkCmdBeginRendering` (command buffer ends, descriptor pool is reset). Now `draw` checks before the descriptor writes that 96 MB of ring space is free, otherwise `flush()`. Afterwards 170 s without crash, 725 k draws.
    3. **Correction:** The window after the loading bar still shows *the loading screen* of the contract scene: black background, bar full, subtitles "Good. All signed and sealed." → "Now, let's begin the transfusion. Oh, don't you worry." (window screenshots; the lines change; 205–344 s unchanged on the last one). The game has **not** arrived in the dream. Before that (≈ 115 s) the loading screen shows the item description ("Bloodshot Eyeball") correctly rendered – book background, text, icon – and fades out at ≈ 155 s.
    4. **Texture/shader gaps (each log + run):** 2D macro tiling (`src/gpu/tiling.h`: pipe/bank interleave, tile index 10–12, 14–18, one sample, level 0, bank swizzle 0; test evidence `test_gcn`: bijection element↔byte for all modes and 8–128 bpp, **no** comparison with reference data), texture format 10_11_11/11_11_10 float (B10G11R11), `s_addk_i32`/`s_mulk_i32` (without SCC), `v_addc_co_u32`/`v_subb(rev)_co_u32`, `v_lshl(rev)_b64`, `v_writelane_b32`/`v_readlane_b32` (spill pattern only, same lane index), `image_sample_d`, `image_sample_c{,_b,_l,_lz}` (S# compare function → Vulkan compare sampler, sampler key extended by the function). No longer appearing in the log after the change: tiling 14, dfmt 6/nfmt 7. Still open: backward jumps (loops, 5 shaders), `s_load` data as shader value (3), `buffer_atomic_add`, `ds_append`, `buffer_*_format` dfmt 0/6, tiling 0/19, primitive 9, `image_sample_c_d`/cube gradients. **Not verified:** whether the 2D macro detiling is bit-exact (reference comparison with real tiling missing; loading-screen textures go through linear/1D and were already visible before).
    5. **Frame time of the loading scene:** on average the main thread waits ≈ 150 ms/frame (≈ 7 fps) for the GXRenderThread; `sceGnmSubmitCommandBuffers` blocks on average ≈ 150 ms (max ≈ 820 ms, `BB_TRACE_SLOW=<ms>` logs HLE calls above the threshold). Backend `draw` ≈ 0.85 ms/draw at ≈ 8.6 k draws/s (the whole world runs in the background behind the loading screen). The texture hash over the entire texture memory per lookup (152 s of 195 s `draw` time) has been replaced: once per frame (`backend_new_frame()` from the flip hook), or after CPU/write-back writes (`g_tex_epoch`) a sample over three 4 KB spans, full hash on change and every 64 epochs (texture time 152 s → 9 s per 240 s run). Remainder: pipeline key strings, `vkCmdBeginRendering` and `barrier()` per draw, descriptor set allocation, 198 GB buffer copies (8 M copies, V# under 64 KB) and 5.7 GB CPU copy/fill of the Gnm helper shaders per 240 s. Profile of the render-thread wait time: ≈ 47 % `NtWaitForMultipleObjects` (fence), ≈ 19 % large `memset`/`memcpy`.
    6. **Correction to items 6/8 (evidenced by a 470 s run without GPU and a 600 s GPU run):** There is no hang. The game *keeps loading and gets into the game world*: after the voice lines of the contract scene (voice streams `sprj_sm24_eng.fsb`, ≈ 4 s per line) it opens the Hunter's Dream objects (`o240003.objbnd.dcx`, `m24_01_00_00_001201.flver.dcx` …) at ≈ 200 s and writes the first autosave (`/savedata0/userdata0000`, open flags `0x201`) at ≈ 388 s. The long pauses in between are the timer-driven phases of the scene at real-time clock rate, not a missing event. **M3 criterion reached: Hunter's Dream loads, the HUD (health/stamina bars, items, 300 Blood Echoes, Insight 0) appears in the window.** The 3D world in the window is still black or grey.
    7. New debug aids: `BB_TRACE_HLE_AFTER=<s>` (only `sce*` calls without mutex/rwlock/Gnm after a given time), `BB_TRACE_SLOW=<ms>`, `BB_PROFILE_FROM=<s>` (profile window), the profiler resolves DLL samples as `module!export` and also prints the stack caller (`[rsp]`, often unusable), crash report shows host stack words in the exe, `BB_GPU_LOG` shows time/submits and counters (copy bytes, texture/descriptor time, zero binds, RT/texture count). Pitfall: `dbghelp` also labels `memset` as `memmove+0x…` after `memmove`. Window screenshots: `computer.window({title:"BloodbornePC"})` in eval.
    8. **Stall analysis without GPU backend (`BB_NO_GPU=1`, ≈ 100 frames/s main loop):** The loading screen hangs *even without a renderer* – so it is not a GPU problem. Timeline (`BB_TRACE_IO`): `sprj_sm24_eng.fsb` is opened several times at ≈ 96–118 s, then **≈ 78 s with no file access** (all workers idle; main thread, MOMainThread and GXRenderThread spin in idle loops: MOMainThread waits on frame semaphore `1000041970`, which the main thread signals ≈ 100×/s; FMOD mixer runs 4× Ajm jobs per 21 ms), then at ≈ 197 s it opens `sprj_sm24_eng.fsb` again and at ≈ 202 s `sprj_xm24.fsb`; MOMainThread signals the FMOD command semaphore `1000e259f0` only every ≈ 4 s during the run. The wait is timer-driven, not event-driven. Ruled out (each measured): `usleep` granularity (now `precise_sleep_us`, high-resolution timer; effect on the gap: none), audio pacing (`audio_output` now per absolute deadline), NP/NetCtl callbacks (`sceNpRegisterStateCallback`/`sceNetCtlRegisterCallback` now report "signed out/disconnected" once via `call_guest`; effect: none). Ajm: ATRAC9 jobs now only consume whole superframes (`in_size/superframe` or output space), channel count/sample rate come from the ATRAC9 config (`fe7004f0` = 1 ch, `fe7209f0`, `fe7a13f0`), `total_decoded_samples` is cumulative per instance – effect on the gap: none. **Superseded by item 6:** there is no hang, the phases are timer-driven; the lack of effect from the fixes is therefore expected (they remain as correctness improvements).
    9. `ctest --test-dir build`: 7/7 green after these changes.
    10. **Graphify (knowledge graph over `src`, `tests`, `tools`; local, `graphify-out/` is ignored):** 76 code files, 1810 nodes, 4149 edges, 93 communities (AST only, no LLM). Build/update: `python tools/graph.py` (`build/` code is excluded, ≈ 3 s); queries: `graphify query "<question>"`, `graphify explain "<node>"`, `graphify path "<A>" "<B>"`. Community names are deterministic (main file + node with highest degree); the graph does not know the *generated* game code – `bbgame.map` and the trace build remain responsible for that. Use in this session: call/ownership map of the HLE layer (`graphify explain "sema_wait()"` led directly to `thread.cpp:243`), god nodes `Context`, `ret()`, `Backend`, `Xlat`.
    11. **Render state of the game world (RT dump `BB_GPU_STATS=1` + `BB_GPU_DUMP=<dir>`, conversion to PNG in eval):** the geometry pass renders the scene's **G-buffer** correctly (normals, albedo, material; operating table, doctor silhouette, shelves, windows; 1920×1088). The HDR lighting result is very dark, the display buffer shows only a grey area with HUD. Artifacts in the G-buffer: green/noisy vertical stripes on individual meshes (tablecloth, shelf wall); the textures themselves are not the cause (`BB_TEX_DUMP=<dir>` writes all de-tiled textures, checked with a DDS header in Pillow: all 1D-thin textures BC1/BC4/BC7 with 1024–4096 px correct; 99 % of textures are tile index 13, only 11 are index 14). Suspicion: vertex data (skinning/cloth, `buffer_*_format` dfmt 0/6 of the Gnm helper shaders) or missing lighting compute – **not verified**.
    12. **Loops in the shader translator (`src/gpu/gcn_spirv.cpp`: `scan_loops`/`begin_loop`/`end_loop`/`break_loop`):** backward `s_branch`/`s_cbranch_*` are turned into structured SPIR-V loops (header at jump target, continue block, exit = forward jump after the back edge). One loop per invocation (exec masking in the body is preserved); at the header, known constants and `exec == all` are discarded; descriptor registers may not change within the loop. Evidence: 244 of 254 shaders in the corpus translate and validate (`bbspv`), all 5 compute shaders that previously failed on loops (copy loops with `image_store`) translate. Also new: `s_cmpk_*`, `v_mad_i32_i24`/`v_mul_i32_i24`. Limitation: cross-lane operations in loops are only exact with a uniform iteration count. Open: loop with a changing descriptor register (1 PS), `v_writelane` data from `s_load` (2), `buffer_atomic_add`, `ds_append`, `buffer_*_format` dfmt 0/6 (2 Gnm helper shaders).
    13. **Further translator functions and findings on the dark lighting pass:** `s_load` data as a runtime value (new resource type `Resource::load_data`: `eval_resources` synthesizes a V# over the loaded dwords, the shader reads them from a storage buffer; descriptor use stays symbolic), `v_writelane_b32`/`v_readlane_b32` exact for constant lane indices (VOP2 encoding: the 8-bit src1 field is an SGPR/constant code, the disassembler shows it as `v128`; one side variable per (VGPR, lane)), `buffer_atomic_{add,sub,smin,umin,smax,umax,and,or,xor,swap}` (32-bit, `glc` returns the old value). Still failing after that: `v_mbcnt_hi_u32_b32` (2 compute shaders with wave-wide atomics aggregation, needs real wave semantics), `ds_append` (GDS counter), one PS with `s_load` base from a computed pointer. **Primitive type 9 (= patch/tessellation) is skipped:** `skip_by prim` = 62,627 of 1.43 M draws in 240 s (each frame repeats the same VS/PS combination, `n=299…301`); the associated vertex shaders (3 of them) read from LDS via `ds_read2_b32`, i.e. the domain stage of an LS→HS→VS tessellation chain. The lighting result remains dark despite the new functions; tessellation (HS/LDS) is the largest known remainder, evidence for the link to the lighting is missing.
    14. **Depth snapshots, 3D textures, diagnostic tools (evidence: RT/depth dumps, `BB_OPS_LOG`):** each frame the game copies the depth buffer (`0x10535f0000`, 2048×1152 `D32S8`) via Gnm helper compute into plain memory (`0x106e1d0000`), which later passes sample as a texture (1920×1080, tile index 0); likewise render targets (`0x1072290000 → 0x1076500000` …). Previously the copy path was rejected (`image copy not handled`), the texture stayed empty. Now `image_cs_op` registers the target as a snapshot image at the target address (texture accesses alias RTs by address), depth is copied as `R32_SFLOAT` via a buffer detour and refreshed on **every** copy (the first copy sat on empty depth), with a mismatched descriptor size (1920×1080 from 2048×1152) `get_texture` returns a cropped copy. Also: tile index 19 (`Thick1DThick`, volume textures; `micro_pixel_index_thick`, not checked against reference data), R11G11B10 fill, `Image::snapshot/version/crop_of`. Still dark: even with current depth, the lighting pass (`0x1077ce8000`/`0x10774f0000`, ≈ 170 fullscreen draws per frame with `vs_8747a367`) shows almost only black with vertical stripes; `BB_ZTEST_OFF=1` (depth test always passed for read-only draws) does not change it; the display chain ends in a grey area + HUD. **Open/not verified:** whether the stripes come from the 2D macro detiling (not checked bit-exact), a viewport/scissor problem of the lighting quads (`PA_SC_VPORT_SCISSOR` per quad, e.g. `82800180/03000200`), or vertex data.
    15. **New diagnostic variables (all debug only):** `BB_OPS_LOG=<first>,<count>` (per draw/dispatch shader, target, result `recorded`/`skip:<reason>`), `BB_LOG_RT=<hex>[,<skip>]` (depth/scissor/viewport state of the first 24 draws to a target), `BB_DUMP_IMG=<hex>` (with `BB_LOG_RT` + `BB_GPU_DUMP`: the same image before each logged draw), `BB_SNAP_LOG=1` (statistics of each depth snapshot), `BB_ZTEST_OFF=1`, `BB_TEX_DUMP=<dir>`; `BB_GPU_STATS` now also writes `ds_<addr>_<w>x<h>.raw` (depth as float32); `BB_GPU_LOG` reports created/newly created depth buffers and textures with unsupported tiling, including address and size. Conversion of raw dumps to PNG and contact sheets via Python (`numpy`/`Pillow`) in eval, not in the repo.
    16. **The game world is visible in the window (evidence: window screenshot after ≈ 225 s, O2, pad script).** Three causes, each evidenced by a dump: (a) **Constant Engine:** the engine fills descriptor tables (T#/V#/S#) via the CCB (`WRITE_CONST_RAM` 0x81, `DUMP_CONST_RAM` 0x83); the draw buffer waits via `WAIT_ON_CE_COUNTER` (0x86) for `INCREMENT_CE_COUNTER` (0x84) of the CCB. The CCB was previously ignored (tables sat at `0xFFFFFFFF`, hence "image type 0/2 not implemented" in the post-processing shaders, `skip:shader`). Now the CCB runs lazily: each wait packet instance lets it run until the next counter increment, the rest at the end of the submit (`ce_advance`, `g_ceq`, `src/hle/gnm.cpp`; ponytail: `WAIT_ON_DE_COUNTER_DIFF`/`INCREMENT_DE_COUNTER` are ignored, since the CCB only runs on demand; `LOAD_CONST_RAM` 0x80 is implemented, did not occur in 170 s). (b) **Constant buffers in the VS were clamped to the number of drawn vertices** (`size = min(size, cur_vtx_records * stride)` applied to every VS V# with a stride, including constant buffers; a fullscreen triangle read the scale factor beyond 48 bytes as 0 → degenerate triangle, the entire post-processing chain drew nothing, the display image was the grey clear color). New `Resource::scalar` (read via `s_buffer_load`) disables the clamp. (c) `DMA_DATA` accepts the L2 selectors (`src/dst_sel` 3). Result: the window shows the operating table, shelves, arches and the figure of Hunter's Dream – **dark, with tile artifacts** (ghost blocks, flat bluish areas, stripes); lighting/post chain are not yet bit-exact. ctest 7/7 green. New debug aid `BB_SPV_PROBE=<shader-crc hex>:<pc hex>:<vgpr>` (a PS exports v[n..n+2] as color before this instruction, alpha 1; readable with `BB_LOG_RT`/`BB_DUMP_IMG`), `BB_LOG_RT=<hex>[,<first draw>]` now counts from the global draw index and also logs T#/V# (with written dword ranges), index buffer, PS user data and table.
    17. **Audio (evidence: `BB_AJM_LOG`/`BB_AUDIO_LOG` runs, 150 s):** libSceAjm now decodes ATRAC9 for real via LibAtrac9 (MIT, Alex Barney, pinned to commit `efca2e3`, `FetchContent` with `SOURCE_SUBDIR no_cmake`, target `bbatrac9`, one decoder handle per Ajm instance, reset/initialize resets it; output S16/S32/float per instance flags, whole superframes, input/output also as `AjmBuffer` arrays). Observed: mono/stereo streams with peak values 0.04–0.74 (previously silence). `sceAudioOut*` writes into one SDL3 audio stream per port (`src/gpu/audio_out.cpp`, `hle::AudioSink`; only port types MAIN/BGM/PERSONAL, queue capped at ≈ 250 ms, pacing still via absolute deadline); run: ≈ 220 MB written in 150 s, 0 KB discarded, peak level 0.34, no open error. **Not verified:** audibility/sound quality (no listening check during the run), channel order for 8-channel streams, `sceAudioOutSetVolume`, gapless skip/encoder delay in Ajm, MP3/AAC instances (still silent).
    18. **The game is interactive (evidence: key press into the window, screenshot):** after ≈ 360 s the game responds to keyboard input (Enter = Cross: the stamina bar shortens, the image changes). The scene (operating table with doctor) is **very dark**; whether this is the real intro scene or wrong lighting (flat surface colors, tile ghosts in the lighting pass) is not decided. The compute shaders `cs_4ca76892` (TEA random numbers + `v_mbcnt`, particles) and `cs_a954e79d` (`ds_append`) are still not translatable.
    19. **Backend performance (evidence: `BB_GPU_LOG` window 190–230 s, `frames presented` as frame counter, RTX 3070 Ti; entry into Hunter's Dream):** from **7.0 to ≈ 14.8 frames/s** (≈ 1495 draws/frame, 22 k draws/s). Causes and measures, each measured: (a) full-texture hash ran every 64 *epochs*; epochs also count CPU fill/copy ops (≈ 200/s) → ≈ 45 % of render-thread time in `hash_bytes`; now every 64 *frames* (`g_frame_counter`) and with a 4-lane hash (`hash_fast`). (b) **Vertex streams were copied into the upload ring per draw (≈ 400 GB per 230 s)**: now a device-local cache per (base, size) with validation against guest memory (≤ 64 KB full hash, otherwise sample 3×4 KB + full hash every 16 frames; unchanged entries only every 1/4/16 frames, immediately after GPU write-backs; 1 GiB budget, LRU), copies 400 GB → 0.2 GB. Additionally the window starts at the lowest index (`vertexOffset = -min`, only if every VS stream is addressed purely via vertex ID, `Resource::vertex_use`). (c) Several consecutive draws to the same attachments share one rendering instance; barrier only on attachment change, when sampling an image written since the last barrier, and around draws with UAV side effects (`end_pass`, `pass_written`, `sampled`). (d) Pipeline key as 64-bit hash instead of string (the key did not contain the front-face bit), layout cache per (VS, PS). (e) **Two submit slots** (command buffer, fence, descriptor pool, ring half of 224 MB each): the CPU keeps recording while the GPU runs; write-backs and dirty ranges per slot, applied only when the slot completes or when backend read accesses (buffer/texture/index) hit a dirty range in the other slot (`settle_pending`); synchronous flushes 16.8 k → 2.2 k of 46 k. The presentation blit now has a memory dependency on the running backend submission. (f) Helper fill shaders on render targets that cover only the beginning (all-zero) run as buffer→image copy from the null head of the ring. **Open risks (deliberate, `ponytail` in code):** guest code that reads GPU results without a backend read access sees them up to two flushes late (completion labels are written during the PM4 scan); draws that read compute results within the same submission copy stale data (old gap, not new). **Not solved:** 30 frames/s. The render-thread wait for the reused slot is ≈ 29 % of the time, although GPU time per timestamps is only ≈ 12.6 ms/frame: the GPU downclocks under this bursty load (nvidia-smi: 555–1275 MHz, 30 % utilization, P3/P5; under synchronous load 1995 MHz), the driver is the choke point here; still open are descriptor-set cache, draw-state deduplication and the ≈ 8 µs/draw in `write_descriptors`. `ctest` 7/7 green. New debug output: `[draw() sections ms …]`, `[vertex cache …]`, `[flushes …]`, `[buffer copies by kind …]`, `BB_VTX_DBG=1`.
    20. **Linux (evidence: WSL2 Ubuntu, g++ 15.2, CMake 3.31/Ninja from tarball, without root):** `cmake -G Ninja -DCMAKE_BUILD_TYPE=Release` + `ninja` builds all libraries and tools including `bbhle`/`bbrt`/`bbgcn`/`bbatrac9` without errors (only GCC warnings: `-Wpsabi` for AVX return values in `guest.h`, unused variables in `functions.cpp`/`process.cpp`/`crash.cpp`, field initializers in the tools); **`ctest` 7/7 green** (before: 5/7: `recomp_default`/`recomp_bias` crashed with `#GP` because the fixture function `vec_math` executes a 32-byte-aligned `vmovntps ymm` on the glibc heap block, which was only 16-byte-aligned; the test now allocates the guest memory 64-byte-aligned, MSVC still green). The first generated shard (`shard_0000.cpp`, 9.3 MB) translates under `g++ -std=c++20 -mavx -O1` in 34 s without errors. **Not evidenced / open:** the game build itself (`bbgame`) is still MSVC-only (`CMakeLists.txt`: `if(BB_EBOOT AND MSVC)`, `src/app/main.cpp` uses `windows.h`/`VirtualAlloc`, `/MAP`, `/arch:AVX`); GCC/Clang branches are missing, a run of the remaining 88 shards (≈ 17 min expected at `-j 3`), SDL3 with X11/Wayland headers (only the runtime libraries are installed in WSL; `apt-get download` works without root, `.deb` can be unpacked with `dpkg -x`), and any run under Linux (Steam Deck never tested).
    21. **Frame rate and output resolution (evidence: `BB_PRESENT_LOG`, RTX 3070 Ti, Hunter's Dream):** `src/gpu/present.cpp` is new: every flipped guest frame is copied into one of 3 snapshot ring buffers, the window shows `mix(previous, newest, α)` with α = time since arrival / interval between the last two arrivals (VK fullscreen-triangle pipeline, SPIR-V via `SpvBuilder`). `BB_FPS=native|30|60|120|144|unlimited` (default `native` = FIFO, one present per guest frame; otherwise MAILBOX/IMMEDIATE and pacing via sleep+spin), `BB_INTERP=0` disables the blending. **Measured:** `60` → 60.0 frames/s shown at 14.5 guest frames/s; `144` → 144.0; `unlimited` → ≈ 7600 (guest frame rate in the short runs 30/s, in the game world 14.5/s); screenshot of the game world via the new path intact (HUD, scene as before). **Honest limits:** (1) This is intermediate-frame blending of the *output image*, not "fixed sim tick + interpolation": game speed, physics, animation, AI and input run unchanged at the pace of the guest engine (here limited by backend performance to ≈ 14.5/s); the display lags one guest frame behind and real motion-vector interpolation is missing (pure crossfade → ghosting on fast motion). Higher true simulation rates would require intervening in the guest's engine frame-timing logic (not started). (2) `BB_WINDOW=WxH` (e.g. `3840x2160`) and F11 fullscreen set the window size; swapchain 3840×2160 confirmed by log (the test screen has only 1920×1080, so image quality at 4K is therefore not evidenced by screenshot). The **internal render resolution of the guest stays 1920×1080** (Bloodborne renders at a fixed 1080p; upscaling of the render targets is not implemented) – the image is scaled bilinearly to the window size.
    22. **Corrections after review (each checked against the code):** (a) The interpolation α was computed before the new image was captured → on the first frame of each new pair α≈1, afterwards small (forward jump, backward blend); now computed after capture in `present_one`; `BB_PRESENT_ALPHA=1` logs the sequence; observed: after each `NEW`, α rises monotonically from 0 (0.036 → 0.072 → …). (b) Draws that write into guest memory via a host import (`get_host_buf`) had no write-back, but the completion label goes out immediately during the PM4 scan → such submits are synchronous again (`imp_written`, counter `imported writes` in `BB_GPU_LOG`; 17.6 k of 48 k flushes). Measured: frame rate unchanged at ≈ 14.9/s (measurement window 190–242 s). (c) `audio_write` held the stream pointer without a lock → `audio_close` could destroy it under the SDL calls; lock now spans both calls. (d) LibAtrac9 reads up to 4 bytes past the current position (no length parameter) → input superframe is copied into a buffer padded by 8 bytes. (e) `tests/test_spirv.cpp`: regression test that `s_buffer_load` sets `scalar` and a vertex fetch does not. Not adopted: the VS-clamp note (`!res[i].scalar` is already in the clamp) and the timer note (measurement, no change). `ctest` 7/7 green.
    23. **Linux game build (evidence: WSL2 Ubuntu, g++ 15.2, `-DBB_EBOOT=… -DSDL_UNIX_CONSOLE_BUILD=ON`; replaces the "not evidenced" list from item 20 for `bbgame`):** `CMakeLists.txt` now also builds `bbgame` with GCC/Clang (`-mavx`, `-O2`/`-O0` per `BB_GAME_OPT`, `-w` for the shards, no `/MAP`/`/bigobj`), `src/app/main.cpp` reserves guest memory via `hle::host_map` (VirtualAlloc/mmap) and `windows.h` only under `_WIN32`. **Evidence:** all 89 shards + `bbgpu` + `bbgame` compile and link without errors (≈ 10 min at `-j 3`, `-O0`). **First Linux run** (`BB_HEADLESS=1 BB_NO_GPU=1`) aborted in DT_INIT with a libstdc++ assertion: `std::atomic_ref` requires natural alignment, but the guest also locks unaligned operands → `runtime/guest.h` now uses `AtomicRef` on `__atomic` builtins outside MSVC (under MSVC unchanged `std::atomic_ref`). Afterwards the guest runs **100 s without crash** through startup (logos, sound banks `sprj_*.fsb` via `BB_TRACE_IO`, 2257 flips; the buffers are empty without GPU, as expected). **Not evidenced:** window/Vulkan/audio under Linux (in WSL X11/Wayland headers and a Vulkan driver are missing; SDL built without window backend), Steam Deck, a run with GPU. Windows build unchanged (`AtomicRef` is an alias there).
    24. **Retry shader translation (evidence: `BB_GPU_LOG` run 240 s):** failed translations were permanent per shader key ("sticky"). Errors that depend on descriptor contents (`image type 0` = T# still empty at first draw, `buffer format dfmt=…`, `unreadable`) are now re-translated on every 128th use (`ShaderEntry::fail_uses`; helper CS with `kind` excluded). Observed: `ps@105b20fa00` (alpha pass of the HDR chain, mask 8) and `cs@10d5e00900` translate afterwards. The scene stays dark (brightened ×8 visible: garden with ivy, arches, silhouette – geometry is there, exposure is missing; in the light RT often only a directional-light-like constant color remains). **Ruled out:** shadow map (the 4096² depth draws run: `dctl=0x700737`, PS present, no shader errors; `BB_LOG_Z=<hex base>` logs all draws to a depth buffer). **Still open, not proven:** (a) 161 k tessellation draws (`prim 9`, G-buffer pass `zwrite=0x10535f00`, 8 targets) are skipped → geometry missing in the G-buffer; (b) GDS/`ds_append` and `v_mbcnt` CS (indirect dispatch arguments, particles) are skipped; (c) LDS (`ds_read2_b32`) is missing in 3 VS and in the translator generally.
    25. **Fast clear (CMASK) and tessellation – findings and status (evidence: `BB_GPU_WATCH`, `BB_PIXEL`, self-screenshots `BB_SHOT_AT`):** (a) **Correction of the measurement method:** screen grabs (`ImageGrab`) return foreign windows as soon as another window lies over the game (several earlier "screenshots" were the user's game) – the authoritative source is now `BB_SHOT_AT=<seconds>:<file.ppm>` (writes the game's own snapshot frame; the same findings with title bar/HUD remained valid). (b) **Fast clear was the cause of the "tile ghosts/green areas":** the game clears G-buffer targets by setting their **CMASK** (e.g. `0x1072a88000`, 0x5000 bytes) to 0 with the fill-helper CS (`BB_GPU_WATCH` shows 4432 such fills/215 s) and later draws a `CB_COLOR_CONTROL.MODE = 2` (eliminate fast clear) rect without PS (previously skipped as `no_target`). For uncovered pixels we kept the old memory contents (garbage: `0,241,0` among others). Now: a fill with 0 over a known CMASK address marks the target in advance (`cmask_rt`, `fast_clear_pending`); the next draw into it first clears the image to `CB_COLOR*_CLEAR_WORD` (8-bit RGBA decoded, otherwise 0; `fast clears` in `BB_GPU_LOG`; ponytail: CMASK/DCC metadata itself is not emulated). Result (self-screenshot): the green artifacts are gone, the fog sky shows up correctly bright, **but the room environment is completely missing** (black silhouettes) – previously stale frames had filled the gaps. (c) **Missing geometry = tessellation:** ≈ 47 patch draws (`prim 9`, 1 control point per patch, quad domain, `VGT_TF_PARAM=0x42`) per frame in the G-buffer pass. The driver-side functions `sceGnmSetLsShader`/`SetHsShader` were NOP stubs → LS/HS registers were missing; now they generate `SET_SH_REG` for PGM/RSRC and carry the raw register block in a NOP payload (`BBTS` magic) into virtual SH slots `0x3C0`/`0x3E0` (`HS regs`: `[5]/[6]` = `VGT_HOS_MAX/MIN_TESS_LEVEL`, `[7]` input control points). The disassembly (`BB_SHADER_DIR` now dumps `hs_`/`ls_`) shows: the **HS stores only constant tess factors** (edges = max level, inside = min level, as constants in the code), the **LS** writes 30 dwords per control point (128-byte record at `v1<<7`) into LDS, the **DS** (shader type 8 in the VS slot) reads them back via `ds_read2_b32`. Emulation: `Backend::tess_begin` – LS as compute pass over all control points into a global "LDS" buffer (`tess_lds`, `tess_idx`), then DS as a normal VS over a generated (N+1)² grid per patch (instance = patch, synthetic index buffer, `DrawCmd::host_indices`); translator side (`GcnEnv::tess_ls/tess_ds_level`, `Resource::LdsBuffer/TessIndices`) by a subagent, see below. **Not yet evidenced:** that the patch draws bring the environment into the image (see follow-up entry, if present). Further debug variables of this round: `BB_GPU_WATCH=<hex>` (every GPU-side write action to an address), `BB_PIXEL=<rt>:<x>:<y>:<from draw>` (which draw/dispatch changes a pixel), `BB_LOG_Z=<hex>`, `BB_GPU_DUMP_ONLY=<hex,…>`, `BB_TESS_LOG`. Also from this round's review: `COMPUTE_PGM_RSRC2.LDS_SIZE` (bits 23:15, 512-byte units on Sea Islands) now sets `GcnEnv::lds_bytes` for compute shaders (LDS accesses of the CS were previously rejected as "translated without LDS").
    26. **Tessellation runs without crash but is still opt-in (`BB_TESS=1`; evidence: `bbspv`, ctest, run 235 s):** the first LS module was invalid SPIR-V (`OpSelectionMerge` not directly before its branch, plus `gl_Position` in the interface of a compute module) and made the NVIDIA driver crash in `nvoglv64.dll`. Fixed in `Xlat::setup_ls` (comparison before the merge) and without position output for LS (exports in LS fail cleanly). `bbspv --tess-ls [--fetch-stub] <dump.bin>` / `--tess-ds N <dump.bin>` translates and validates game shaders without a game run: both LS and all three DS of the corpus are `valid`. `tests/test_spirv.cpp` checks LS/DS (entry model, resource order); the helper functions now walk the instruction stream instead of expecting a fixed word count. `ctest` 7/7. **In game** (`BB_TESS=1`): no crash in 235 s, no more `tess` skips; **but** only ≈ 470 k instead of ≈ 3.3 M draws in 230 s (the game runs ≈ 7× slower) and in the self-screenshot no additional geometry is reliably recognizable. Cause and remedy: see item 27 (tessellation has become the default there).
    27. **Tessellation on by default, layered RTs, background test mode (evidence: self-screenshots, G-buffer dumps, ctest 7/7):** (a) The LS compute pass bound its vertex streams in full; now only up to the highest control-point ID (`cur_vtx_records`). Throughput with tessellation 2.8 M instead of 0.47 M draws in 230 s (without: ≈ 3.3 M). The G-buffer thus contains the **room geometry** for the first time (floor, walls, arches, shelves; previously black). Tessellation is therefore now **default** (`BB_TESS=0` disables it). Open: vertical stripes at the image edges in the G-buffer, doctor silhouette black. (b) **Layered render targets:** the point-light shadow cubemap `0x1059280000` (256², 6 faces) is drawn face by face via `CB_COLOR_VIEW` (SLICE_START/SLICE_MAX = face index) (the array is allocated with a multiple of 6 slices right away); we had created a single 2D image and bound it as a 2D view to a cube sampler. Now: `get_rt(..., layers)` (2D array, CUBE_COMPATIBLE for 6n square slices), attachment view per slice (`attach_view`), `texture_view` selects the view type (cube/2D) from the T# for layered RTs. Observed: cube is created with 6 slices. Image brightness not visibly changed by this. (c) **Exposure (cause of the darkness, partial finding):** `ps_5c02eaf8` is a filmic tonemapper: color ≈ HDR-derived · s4 · v5..v7 → `1-exp(-k·x)` (reading of the disassembly). Probe (`BB_SPV_PROBE=5c02eaf8:88:5`) shows **v5..v7 = 0** across the whole image (result of the 4-channel `image_sample_lz` at 0x48); which T# feeds these registers (a 1×1 texture or e.g. bloom) is **not verified** – the disassembly operands of the SRSRC do not match the `s_load` targets. The shader's 1×1 textures lie in a ring that is newly allocated for each frame (`0x105b16db00`, `0x105b200f00`, …); according to `BB_GPU_WATCH` no GPU operation writes them, according to `BB_TINY_LOG` they are not RTs, their memory contains pointers (like a descriptor table). Next step: determine whether the translator's T# resolution (load/word) for this binding is wrong, or whether the texture comes from a pass that did not run. (d) **Background test mode** `BB_BACKGROUND=1` (the user plays in parallel): small window 480×270 without focus (`SDL_WINDOW_NOT_FOCUSABLE`), no gamepad subsystem, no sound; test runs with `BELOW_NORMAL` priority, builds via `start /low` with `-j 2`. New debug variables: `BB_SPV_DUMP=<dir>`, `BB_TINY_LOG=<from draw>`.
    28. **Cause of the darkness found: SSAO pass was missing (evidence: probes in the light shader, pixel history, self-screenshot):** (a) Measured: the tonemap constant `s4` (cbuffer dword 0x268) is 1.5, so not an exposure error; the HDR scene image itself is ≈ 100× too dark. (b) The main light shader `ps_d92266b9` multiplies the diffuse part by the Z component of a half-resolution texture (`T#` `0x107b5c0000`, 960×540). Probe directly after the sample (`BB_SPV_PROBE=d92266b9:88:15`): **x = z = 0, w = 1** across the whole image. This texture is written by `ps@10d5fe7300` (SSAO, 8 iterations with `s_buffer_load` at a loop-dependent offset) – the shader was **never translated** with "loop changes a descriptor register", the draw was skipped. (c) Fix in the translator: a loop may change a register that previously held a descriptor, as long as the body does not use its symbolic value (`Loop::sym_reads`, set in `desc_ref`, the `s_load` base and symbolic `rd_s` reads; changed registers are unknown afterwards; nested loops inherit the read set). The SSAO shader now translates; **mean image brightness 9 → 31 (of 255)**, room and shelves are visible in the light. `ctest` 7/7. (d) Open, next cause: **green areas** (≈ 2.9 % of pixels, `0,249,0,0` in the G-buffer targets `0x1072a90000`/`0x1073a90000`) and noisy rectangles at the image edges. The associated pixel history could not be measured because the user was playing in parallel (runs reached only 0.26–0.46 M instead of 2.8 M draws). (e) The disassembler showed the MIMG `SRSRC` twice (unit 8 instead of 4 SGPRs) – display only, the translation was correct; fixed. New debug variables: `BB_VLOG_OFF=<hex dword>` (V# data from offset), `BB_LOG_RT_N=<n>`, 4th field at `BB_SPV_PROBE` (scaling); `BB_PIXEL` now also reads R11G11B10/R32F/BGRA8.
    29. **Alpha test (pixel kill) was missing – cause of the green areas (evidence: pixel history, self-screenshot, ctest 7/7):** The cloth/foliage shader `ps_1c3953c6` (alpha-tested) wrote `0,249,0,0` into the G-buffer at a green pixel. It clears discarded lanes from its saved EXEC mask and restores them immediately before the last export (`exp mrt0 … done vm`); on GCN, EXEC at this VM export is the validity mask – pixels with a cleared bit are discarded for **all** MRTs. The translator predicated only the stores; the fragment then wrote undefined outputs. Now: at the VM export in the PS, `OpKill` for lanes without the EXEC bit (`Xlat::exp`). Result: the green areas are gone, floor and walls show warm light. **Open:** streaky pattern on the lit floor, noisy blocks at the left image edge, doctor silhouette black. (b) **Test runs without disturbing the user:** `BELOW_NORMAL` priority starved the game behind background apps (21 k instead of 150 k draws after 100 s); the pad script ran on wall-clock time and missed the menus. Now `BB_PAD_CLOCK=flips` (pad script driven by guest flips/30 instead of wall clock, ponytail: reference at 30 flips/s) – the game world is thus reached even in slow runs; `build/rungame.py` picks `NORMAL` only below 30 % CPU load, otherwise `BELOW_NORMAL`. (c) A run crashed in `pm4_execute` while reading an unmapped PM4 stream (`0xc0055000`); streams are now checked against `mem_valid` before parsing and otherwise skipped (cause of the wrong pointer unknown).
    30. **SGPR spills carry symbols (evidence: run 345 s, A/B with `BB_SKIP_PS`):** `ps@10d7d5e200` (35 k SPIR-V words, 30 resources; apparently the character shader) was never translated with "s_load base pointer is not user-data derived": it saves its descriptor-table pointers via `v_writelane_b32` and fetches them back via `v_readlane_b32`; the symbol was lost in the process. Now `Xlat::spill_sym_` records the symbol of the saved SGPR per (VGPR, lane) and restores it on fetch-back. The shader translates; in the self-screenshot the doctor is no longer a black silhouette (A/B at the same time point via `BB_PAD_CLOCK=flips`: mean brightness 17.9 with vs. 15.8 without). New debug variable `BB_SKIP_PS=<hex>` (deliberately skip one PS). **Observation:** between runs the image content differs (camera/light flicker in the intro); judge brightness comparisons only as A/B within the same run.
    31. **GDS counters, indirect draws/dispatches, EOS labels (evidence: run 300 s with `BB_GPU_LOG`):** `ds_append … gds` is now translated (`Xlat::gds`, resource `GdsBuffer` = 64 KiB at the end of the upload ring, address M0[15:0]+offset; per lane one atomic minus lane index, because the shader afterwards adds `v_mbcnt`). `cs@10d95ef100`, `cs@10d9620400`, `cs@10d961fd00` (particle simulation) translate. `DMA_DATA` with GDS end (observed: counter saved/reloaded at GDS 0x20/0x24) runs as `vkCmdCopyBuffer` in stream order (`hooks().gds_copy`). Newly handled: `SET_BASE` (index 1), `DISPATCH_INDIRECT` (0x16), `DRAW_INDIRECT` (0x24) – previously silently ignored; arguments observed e.g. dispatch 3×1×1, draw 4 vertices/1 instance; before reading, pending GPU writes are waited for (`hooks().sync_read`, ponytail: synchronous). `EVENT_WRITE_EOS` command 2 now writes its label (previously ignored). Unhandled PM4 opcodes are logged once; remaining: 0x3C WAIT_REG_MEM, 0x46, 0x58, 0x85, 0x88.
    32. **Cube maps with 6 faces – cause of the colorful noise blocks (evidence: pixel history, texture dump, self-screenshot):** the blocks came from G-buffer shaders (`ps@10d7e73200`) that read a BC6H cube map (128², reflection) via `image_sample_l`. Its T# has DEPTH 0 and names the faces only via LAST_ARRAY (word 5 [25:13] = 5); `get_texture` therefore created a cube map with one layer. Now: layers = max(DEPTH, LAST_ARRAY+1), rounded to 6 for cubes. The blocks are gone from the screenshot; the textures themselves (BC1/BC4/BC6H, tiling 13) decode correctly (checked offline via a DDS wrapper).
    33. **Still open after that:** image still too dark, vertical stripes, gray horizontal bars (y≈820 and bottom edge), reddish line (y≈460). Debug aids: `BB_PIXEL` now also reads RGBA16F/RG32F and starts time-based via `BB_PIXEL_AFTER=<s>`; `BB_LOG_RT=<rt>,t<s>` time-based, `BB_LOG_PS=<hex>` logs draws of a PS; `BB_SHADER_DIR` writes `index.txt` (address → file).
    34. **PS inputs routed via `SPI_PS_INPUT_CNTL_n` – largest image error (evidence: draw dumps before/after, self-screenshot):** On GCN, PS input *n* reads the VS parameter `OFFSET[4:0]` from `SPI_PS_INPUT_CNTL_n` (bit 5 = constant `DEFAULT_VAL`, bit 10 = flat); the translator took input *n* = parameter *n*. The diffuse combine `ps@10d5fd3f00` thus read the clip position instead of the UV → light×albedo came out distorted/smeared (horizontal stripes in the left half of the image). Now: `GcnEnv::ps_input_map/ps_input_cntl` (backend fills them from the context registers; the shader cache key includes a hash of the used `CNTL` registers), SPIR-V `Location` = VS parameter, `Flat` for bit 10. Result: light buffer mean 0.008 → 0.05, the HDR image shows the room (table, lanterns, door with figure). Also added `v_interp_mov_f32` (P0, flat) and `v_cvt_f32_f16`/`v_cvt_f16_f32` (`ps@10d9684500` translates). `ctest` 7/7 incl. new flat test.
    35. **HTILE fast clear of the depth buffer (evidence: ops log, depth probe, statistics):** the game does not clear depth/stencil via a clear draw but fills the HTILE buffer (`DB_HTILE_DATA_BASE`, observed: word `0xfffffff0` = ZMASK 0) with the fill helper. Without emulation the depth buffer was never cleared (5 instead of 12,551 DS clears in 300 s): the player stayed black, the G-buffer kept old contents (vertical stripes = remnants of earlier frames). Now: `htile_ds` learns the HTILE→depth buffer mapping from the draws; a fill with ZMASK 0 marks it, and the next draw on it clears it to `DB_DEPTH_CLEAR`/`DB_STENCIL_CLEAR`. Result: player figure with coat/hat in the G-buffer and image, background clean. `BB_PIXEL` now also reads depth buffers (Float32); `BB_OPS_LOG=t<s>,<n>` starts time-based and shows HTILE events.
    36. **Occlusion queries (evidence: event log, G-buffer dump):** `EVENT_WRITE` was unhandled; the game uses `PIXEL_PIPE_STAT_DUMP` (type 0x39) as occlusion query (result blocks 0x90 bytes: 8 RB begin/end pairs + 16 bytes; begin at X, end at X+8). Now 8 valid counters (bit 63, monotonically rising) are written – every query counts as visible (ponytail: no real sample counting). Afterwards the ceiling of the room appears in the G-buffer.
    37. **Walls/floor of the clinic were missing – cause: imprecise interpolation (evidence: A/B without pixel kill, shader analysis, G-buffer dump, screenshot):** the walls were drawn but discarded entirely by the alpha test. The material shaders (e.g. `ps_70bb42af`) read a material ID from `attr0.w`, convert it via `v_cvt_u32_f32` into an index (×13+7) into the material table, and discard the pixel if the parameter read is 0. GCN interpolates `P0 + i·(P1−P0) + j·(P2−P0)` – exact for equal corner values; Vulkan's smooth interpolation yields e.g. 4.9999 → index 4 → wrong entry → everything discarded. Now: with `VK_KHR_fragment_shader_barycentric` (RTX 3070 Ti: available; RADV/Steam Deck: [INFERENCE] supported, unverified) the PS reads the three corner values (`PerVertexKHR`) and computes as GCN does (`GcnEnv::ps_bary`); without the extension the smooth interpolation remains.
