Result: back wall with arches, door, floor, player figure textured; no green alpha artifacts.
    38. **T# base address 38 bits (evidence: log "texture memory not mapped" 239 → 1):** some T# have bit 6 of word 1 set (addresses such as `0x40105b0b3900`); the base is now formed with `w[1] & 0x3F`. As a result, among others, the bloom/HDR mip chain (960×540 … 30×17) and small lookup textures load.
    39. **Open after 38:** image dark (mean ≈ 14/255), bright horizontal bar at y≈820, dark stripe bottom/right, vertical stripes top left, double images. Between runs the exposure changed strongly (beige/fire-red sky/"psychedelic" without walls). 8 frames of a quiet scene showed hardly any difference in the mean – that was no evidence against flicker (measurement by pixel share: see 41). Test runs now open a 1280×720 window (`BB_WINDOW` also applies with `BB_BACKGROUND`).
    40. **Crop padded render targets when sampling (evidence: pixel history, shader analysis, self-screenshot):** render targets are padded to tile size (1920×1088, 1024×576), their texture T# name the image size (1920×1080, 960×540). Normalized UVs ran across the padded image → every post-process pass read slightly stretched/shifted. The bright bar was one such case: the glare upsample `ps@10d5fe4c00` read the 960×540 glare buffer from a 1024×576 image. Now every sample with a smaller T# size gets a cropped copy (previously only for depth snapshots); `Image::version` now counts every write use (color target, storage write, fill/copy) so the copy is re-created (ponytail: full copy per change). Result: bar and dark lower edge gone (the mean of 14 → 26/255 measured back then is no evidence because of the flicker from 41); the clinic is recognizable without brightening (table, infusion bottles, arches, lit door, player figure). Open: vertical stripes on the upper walls, flat wall area behind the arches, empty item slot in the HUD.
    41. **[Solved in 42] Flicker occurs only with the vertex-stream cache – trigger narrowed down (evidence: `BB_SHOT_N=12`, share of changed pixels > 24/255 per frame, two runs each with captures at different times):** previously roughly every 4th–5th frame alternated between images with/without foreground objects (infusion stand, table items), ≈ 21–25 % of the pixels changed, with the same draw count per frame (526). Without cache (`get_vertex_buffer` skipped) 12/12 frames stable (≤ 0.3 % changed pixels) in runs with captures at t=150/200 s and t=288 s; the stable frame shows the full variant (back wall with arches, door, floorboards, player figure). With the cache it still flickers – also with a full hash on every use, and also when streams with GPU write ranges (`overlaps_dirty`) bypass the cache. Discriminating test (cache on, but on every hit upload again into the same persistent buffer; statistics: 0 hits, ≈ 2.17 M uploads): two runs in the clinic (capture at t=200 s and t=288 s), 12/12 frames stable each (≤ 0.2 % changed pixels); an earlier single run with a different scene (mean 87.9) does not count. Control check "hit without upload, but with barrier": still flickers (two runs, 16–20 % changed pixels). So: same buffer, same content per hash – only the hit path without upload flickers; a missing barrier alone is not the cause. Also no effect (two runs, still flickers): the hash of a new entry is now computed over the uploaded ring copy instead of guest memory (guest threads can change `src` between copy and hash; the change stays as a correctness fix). Mechanism still open here (found in 42; the descriptor-pool candidate suspected here was not it). The cache was off by default back then – superseded, on again since 42 (`BB_VTX_CACHE=0` disables it). Cost: ≈ 3450 presented frames in 300 s versus ≈ 4880 with the old (faulty) sampling cache, i.e. **≈ −30 %**; versus the cache with a full hash on every use (≈ 3820) ≈ −10 %. Per A/B **no effect** on the flicker: cropped copies (40), flush before the presenter blit, synchronous submits, host import for read buffers, timing of occlusion-query results, deferred completion labels. An attempt with "GPU-ordered read paths" also removed the flicker, but permanently showed the variant without wall/foreground – reverted. Kept for correctness (not as a flicker fix): completion labels (EOP/EOS/RELEASE_MEM) are written by the backend only after the preceding GPU work (`hooks().label`, `settle()`; submits with labels run synchronously); occlusion queries count as finished and visible from the begin dump. Cropped copies (40): ≈ 50 per frame, ≈ 2.9 GB/s (per-frame cost not cleanly measured by A/B); "shrinking the target to view size" worsened the image and was discarded. Further findings: between runs the clinic walls were sometimes missing entirely (beige background) – not seen again without the cache in two screenshots reviewed, not conclusively checked; a texture with base 0 (784×662, dfmt 6) stays empty. New debug aid `BB_SHOT_N=<n>`.
    42. **Flicker cause found, vertex cache back to default (evidence: `BB_SHOT_N=12`, pixel share > 24/255, two runs each):** (a) Data refuted: a GPU readback of every cache hit (copy into the ring, hash comparison after the fence) showed 1.65 million hits without deviation in two runs – the cache contents were correct. (b) Not a single stream: the cache was stable only for half/a quarter of the streams (by address); forced eviction (budget 128 MB) at half cache likewise; full cache without eviction (6 GB) flickered, full cache in three parallel (slow) runs did too. (c) Discriminating test: full cache plus only ring consumption per hit (without copy/barrier) was stable (two runs) – the additional synchronous ring-wrap flushes (≈ 4500 in 260 s) masked the bug. (d) Cause: `settle_pending` only waited for the **other** slot; reads of guest memory (buffer copies, textures, indices) that GPU work **recorded but not yet submitted** still writes (write-backs/imported writes) read stale data. Now: overlap with the current slot → synchronous flush (counter "still recording", ≈ 51,000 in 300 s), excluding the own write ranges of the draw/dispatch being recorded (`dirty_own`); `hook_sync_read` uses the same path. (e) Also fixed: `ring_alloc` for indices ran **after** `BeginRendering`/`BindPipeline`/`BindDescriptorSets` – a ring-wrap flush there left the draw in a new command buffer without pipeline/pass. Now everything that can flush (descriptor sets, stream/index ring data) happens before the first command of the draw; if it flushes midway, the draw is repeated once in the fresh slot (likewise for dispatches; `tess_begin` waits before its ring allocation). Result with cache on: 12/12 frames stable (≤ 0.1 %) at capture t=200 s and t=288 s, no draws discarded due to flush, ≈ 3640 presented frames in 300 s (without cache beforehand ≈ 3450). Cache is on by default again (`BB_VTX_CACHE=0` disables it). Open: the run with capture at t=288 s again showed the variant without side walls (sky visible) – independent of the flicker, cause open; ponytail: write-backs of an aborted draw attempt are still copied back (ring snapshot taken just before).
    43. **[Cause found in 48] Rare variant "walls/floor white" (evidence: frame count, depth-pixel history, burst captures):** with the status from 42, seen in 2 of ≈ 23 runs (capture t=200 s), not seen in the 18 runs after that – not even in three runs without the debug switch. In the bad run the draw list is the same as in good ones (per frame 521 G-buffer draws, 5,017,470 indices) – so the geometry is submitted; shadows are visible on the white floor. From ≈ 140 s the scene is static (pad script ends) and the image stays the same in one run for over 160 s (burst captures every 8 s) → state per run. In good runs `vs@10d8300900/ps@10d830d100` (3726 indices) and `vs@10d7652c00/ps@10d765c600` write the wall depth at (900,270). Cause open; [INFERENCE] time-dependent (debug load seemed to suppress it, not statistically confirmed). Checked candidate (review note): a flush between the LS pass (`tess_begin`) and the DS draw lets the draw read ring data of the other slot, which the next flush would reuse. Guarded (next flush waits; after two flushes the draw is discarded, counter "patch draws across slots"), measured from the ring allocation in `tess_begin` (i.e. including the LS pass): 0 cases in three runs. **Correction:** the earlier classification "all with walls" rested on a wrong measure (share of nearly white pixels on raw values; the displayed value is ×3) – one run (mean 45) was probably the bad variant. **Addendum (dumps of a good and a bad run):** in the bad run 40 % of pixels have depth 1.0 and empty G-buffers (good: 0.7 %) – walls/floor/ceiling produce no fragments, although the draw list is identical. With `BB_TESS=0` the walls are there (depth 0.996 at (900,300)) → the walls are not tessellation geometry; the tessellation race is ruled out for them. There are partial variants (back wall present, side walls/floor/ceiling missing). Series of 4 runs each (image at 200 s, "bad" = sky instead of walls, by visual inspection): asynchronous label submits (fence watcher) 2/4, synchronous 0/4, watcher without host import (`BB_HOST_IMPORT=0`, new; the player character is missing, ≈ 22 % fewer frames) 0/4, watcher + read-only buffers of all stages as snapshot 0/4, synchronous + snapshot 1/4 – at this base rate statistically not separable, no variant proven as a fix; both changes reverted. Also checked (review note): `sceVideoOutSubmitFlip` reported the flip immediately, although the GPU work of the frame may still be running; waiting for all GPU work before the flip event gave 0/4 and 1/4 (partial variant) at ≈ 6 % fewer frames → no fix, reverted. Rate with synchronous label submits since then: 2 of 24 runs. New debug aid `BB_RES_LOG=<VS address hex>[,<s>]` (every 10 s for one frame: user registers, descriptor words and hash of the guest bytes of each buffer of the draws with this VS – without flush/readback) for the good/bad comparison; in 8 runs with `BB_RES_LOG=10d8300900,190` none was bad. Cause still open. New debug aids: `BB_SHOT_EVERY=<s>` (burst captures `<file>_t<s>.ppm` from `BB_SHOT_AT` on), `BB_GPU_STATS=t<s>` (RT statistics/dump once at this point instead of every 300 submits).
    44. **Engine frame pacing (evidence: `BB_FLIP_STATS` with timestamps, 100 s; primary source on the game: [Kotaku, L. McDonald 2020](https://kotaku.com/how-a-modder-got-bloodborne-running-at-60-fps-1843165797) – according to McDonald the game's hidden 60 fps mode ran "in fast-forward"; whether the logic generally has no frame-time compensation is **not measured** – see 45: the limiter holds a frame time of 1/30 or 1/60 s):** the game sets `sceVideoOutSetFlipRate(0)` (60 Hz) but paces itself to 30 fps: 0–70 s: 300 flips per 10 s, median interval 33.0 ms (p10 30–31 ms). From ≈ 70 s (the clinic) 43–49 ms per frame – [INFERENCE] the game runs slower than real time there, if the logic takes a fixed step per frame (unverified). New: flips complete no earlier than one refresh period (60/(rate+1) Hz) after the previous one (`sceVideoOutSetFlipRate` is stored; `BB_FLIP_PACE=0` disables it) – at 33 ms this does not kick in, flip count in 90 s with/without the same (2420/2419). Cross-check without buffer scan (`BB_FLIP_LOG=1`, only timestamps on flip arrival), 100 s, pacing on/off: 2624/2619 flips, 20–60 s per window median 33.0 ms, p95 35–36 ms, identical – the scan did not distort the measurement, the pacing does not take effect. Consequence for DoD 4: a higher output rate is only possible via intermediate frames (presenter, item 21: cross-fade, no motion interpolation) or via a real frame-time mode in the engine; both open.
    45. **Engine frame limiter found (evidence: call counters per 10 s, return-address samples, recompiled code):** `gettimeofday` is called ≈ 11 million times per second at 0–70 s, almost never in the clinic (frame > 33 ms); 410 of 411 samples come from return address 0x2435e58 in `f_2035ac0` (guest 0x2435ac0, object in `rdi`). There, a mode index (`+0x8`/`+0xc`, switch over 5 cases) sets `+0x10` (interval 2 or 1), `+0x14` and `+0x18` (float frame time 0x3d088889 = 1/30 s; in one case 0x3c888889 = 1/60 s and interval 1 – [INFERENCE] the hidden 60 fps mode); the wait time is computed from `+0x18`, followed by a spin loop over `gettimeofday` (`+0x20` frame start, `+0x28` now, µs) or – depending on a flag – a sleep path (call 0x2fc0368). Consequences: (a) in 30 fps operation the spin burns one core; (b) entry point for DoD 4 (frame time/mode in this object). Open: whether physics/animation/AI read this frame time – test: force `+0x18` to 1/60, or the mode with interval 1, and measure the speed; search for further readers of the constant (e.g. 0x4d298bc). (c) the spin belongs to the performance item (one core permanently occupied).
    46. **Performance: backend thread (evidence: `BB_GPU_LOG` statistics, 200–300 s, clinic; "frames presented"):** (a) Breakdown (200 s): `write_descriptors` ≈ 77 s, of which buffers ≈ 61 s; synchronous flushes wait ≈ 51 s for the GPU (59,420 synchronous flushes, all via `wait=true`: submits with completion labels and read flushes "still recording" ≈ 39,000) – new statistic "synchronous waits". (b) Tried and **reverted**: hash the vertex cache only once per frame (+7 % frames, but risk of missed guest writes within one frame – review objection valid); fence-watcher thread for asynchronous label submits (no measurable gain: 4×215 s in parallel ≈ 2951 vs ≈ 2996 frames synchronous). Kept: exact hash on every use; a key collision bypasses the cache rather than re-creating a buffer that may still be in use. Final status: 3596/3595 frames in 300 s, flicker series ≤ 0.2 %. Next lever: copy reads of GPU-written regions of the ongoing recording on the GPU side instead of waiting synchronously. (c) `rungame.py` copies the save per run: runs had `build/savedata_test` modified by autosave (start state drifted).
    47. **Audio output checked objectively (evidence: `BB_AUDIO_DUMP=<folder>` – new: raw PCM recording of each output stream; in background mode only recorded, not played; run 150 s, pad script):** one stream 48 kHz, 8 channels, float; 228 MB ≈ 149 s, i.e. real time, 0 KB discarded, peak 0.333, no NaN. Silence until ≈ 70 s (logos/menu), then signal on front L/R (RMS 0.01) and channels 6/7 (RMS 0.01–0.04), center and LFE silent; spectral flatness 0.008–0.025 (tonal, no noise), dominant frequencies 65–205 Hz. Listening sample for the user: `build/audio_check_70-140s.wav` (stereo downmix, normalized; local only). Whether it sounds right (music/ambience, no distortion) is therefore **not** established – someone has to listen.
    48. **Cause of "walls/floor/ceiling missing" found (evidence: background hunt `build/hunt.py` with `BB_RES_LOG`/`BB_RES_DUMP` – new: hash or dump of all buffers of the draws of one VS without flush; comparison of good/bad run):** same draws, same indices, same vertex data in the used range, camera constants (864 B) in a bad run byte-identical with the good one – but the 96 B object buffer (`vs r8`) of the wall draw was partly zero (floats 12/22/23 = 0 instead of 0.9962/0.9962/220.33 → degenerate matrix). `BB_GPU_WATCH` on this range: the only writer is the Gnm helper `cs@10d5e00100` (code hash 0xca710881d448644a), a copy kernel driven by a work list (group g reads {target element, source element, last element} and copies last+1 dwords). On the GPU it read its source live via an import – much later than the console would – and its 64 MB target V# marked the whole range as GPU-written. **Fix:** the kernel now runs on the CPU while recording (like the fill/copy helpers; `settle_pending` on the work list, source and target). First version copied one element too few (all 4 runs: wrong camera, player character missing) – corrected to last+1. Result: 4/4 runs show the same correct image (mean 14.5–14.9; previously even the perspective varied between runs), buffer read flushes 29,603 → 0 (200 s), frames 3149 → 3296 (+5 %), synchronous wait 56 → 46 s. Confirmation hunt: **60/60 runs correct** (mean 14.3–15.0; before ≈ 1 of 36 bad, mean 14–79), plus two 12-frame series at 200/288 s stable (≤ 0.1 %). Addendum: group size from `COMPUTE_NUM_THREAD_X` is now masked to bits 15:0 (the upper bits are a separate field); 4 further runs still correct. Not checked: byte-wise comparison of the CPU copy against the GPU result of the same dispatch – the evidence is the image (64/64 runs identical and correct). Also: DMA_DATA, WRITE_DATA, LOAD_CONST_RAM and DUMP_CONST_RAM are executed during parsing and now synchronize via `sync_read` with pending GPU work (2585 overlaps in 200 s – previously unordered). Reverted intermediate attempt: order import reads on the GPU side (showed the variant without walls, fewer frames).
    49. **Performance: write tracking instead of hashing in the vertex cache (evidence: `BB_GPU_LOG` statistics per 200 s, thread CPU times via psutil, series of 4):** measurement before: the render/submit thread is 71 % busy in the clinic (game threads 21 %/15 %), the vertex cache hashed 539 GB in 200 s (≈ 14 GB/s, memory bandwidth) and took 37.6 s. Now: guest memory is allocated under Windows with `MEM_WRITE_WATCH` (`hle::host_map`); `src/gpu/write_watch.{h,cpp}` queries via `GetWriteWatch(RESET)` only the range of an entry and enters written pages with a new epoch into a page table (overlapping entries thereby also see them); GPU writes to imported memory are marked by `settle()` (`ww_mark`). Untouched entries hit without hashing, written ones are hashed (same content still hits); without tracking (Linux) as before. Result: hashed 465 MB instead of 539 GB, vertex-cache time 6.3 s instead of 37.6 s, 3658 instead of 3269 frames in 200 s (+12 %); series of 4: 4/4 correct and identical (≈ 3049 instead of ≈ 2775 frames in 215 s), 12-frame series stable (0.1 %). Self-test `BB_WW_VERIFY=1` (additionally hashes every hit reported as unchanged): 2 runs of 200 s each, 2,226,191 and 2,178,179 checks, **0 deviations**; all guest allocations go through `host_map`→`map_at` (with `MEM_WRITE_WATCH`), ranges spanning two allocations return "not available" (hash). ponytail: the page table grows with every page ever written (≤ guest memory / 4 KB entries). Next big item: synchronous GPU waits (≈ 54 s/200 s).
    50. **Performance: GPU wait time (evidence: `BB_GPU_PROF`, new statistic "GPU time of command buffers" (timestamp per slot) and barrier counter, each 200 s):** the pipelines need only 27.6 s of GPU time, the command buffers in total 45.6 s with 1.58 million full barriers – the synchronous waits (≈ 54 s) are GPU latency/serialization, not load. (a) Vertex-cache uploads via copy-on-write: changed content gets a new buffer (the old one is released after the slot completes); all upload copies run in an upstream command buffer of the same submit with one barrier at the end – no more barriers/pass breaks in the main buffer. Barriers 1.58 → 1.0 million, command-buffer GPU time 45.6 → 41.4 s, frames 3613 → 3665. (b) Chunking: after 256 draws/dispatches the work is submitted without waiting (`BB_CHUNK`, 0 = off); the GPU works in parallel with further recording. A/B with 2 parallel runs each: off 3703 frames/39.0 s wait, 256 → 3827/26.5 s, 128 → 3764/29.1 s, 64 → 3760/23.0 s; under four-way load (4×215 s) ≈ 2924 vs ≈ 3049 in the series of item 49 – no gain demonstrable there. **Therefore opt-in, not default** (`BB_CHUNK=256`); before enabling: 60-run hunt and `BB_WW_VERIFY` with chunking switched on. Also checked (review note "wake-up latency of vkWaitForFences"): 2 ms polling on `vkGetFenceStatus` before the blocking wait, single runs one after another: 4062 frames/22.8 s wait vs 4085/23.0 s without – no effect, reverted. Reference single run (no parallel operation), final status: ≈ 4070 frames in 200 s, synchronous wait ≈ 23 s. Image correct in all runs, 12-frame series 0.0 %. Bugfix on the way: the barrier counter had briefly displaced `pass_written.clear()` (review note), corrected before the measurements.
    51. **Performance: waits and CPU cost of the render thread (evidence: sampling profiler `BB_PROFILE_SECS` + `build/prof.py` (new: resolves RVAs via `bbgame.map`), `BB_GPU_LOG` statistics, nested A/B runs `build/ab_clinic.py` (new), window 120–250 s clinic):** noise between two runs with identical configuration ≈ ±10 % (105/93 and 92/95 ms median). The render thread `GXRenderThread` is ≈ 68 % busy, ≈ 30 % of the time it waits on fences; the main thread ≈ 21 %. (a) **Fence watcher** (thread that checks the fences every 100 µs under the backend lock and writes back/labels immediately; `BB_LABEL_WATCH=0` = old, synchronous): 3/3 rounds faster (91–94 vs 97–101 ms), within noise, kept (labels no longer hang on the next backend activity). (b) `std::getenv("BB_DRAW_LOG")` ran in `log_draw` on every draw/dispatch (strchr/locale lock ≈ 7 % of thread samples) → now static; shader, texture and view keys are numbers instead of `snprintf` strings (own effect not measured); descriptor scratch reused. fps afterwards unchanged within noise. (c) Synchronous waiting on GPU writes into imported memory removed (`BB_IMP_SYNC=1` = old): synchronous wait 28.3 → 5.9 s, in exchange slot wait 14 → 19.6 s; A/B neutral (+11/+1/−1 %). (d) **N submit slots** (`BB_SLOTS=2..4`, default 2): settle now strictly oldest-first, also in the watcher (preparation for N > 2; with 2 slots at most one slot is in flight outside `flush()`, so the old order could not be violated there). A/B 2 vs 3 slots: 80/86, 83/74, 80/81 ms → no demonstrated gain. (e) Remaining: ≈ 6000 forced read flushes per 200 s; new statistic "read flushes by caller" by PM4 command (indirect arguments, WRITE_DATA, DMA, LOAD/DUMP_CONST_RAM). In addition `NtGetWriteWatch` ≈ 7 % of thread samples.
    52. **Frame time of the game logic (evidence: code analysis of the recompiled code, subagent; findings with file:line in `build_o2/gen`):** the game logic gets a **fixed** frame time 1/30 (literal 0x3d088889 in the frame event of `f_201a0d0`, shard 0061), not `+0x18` of the limiter (that is only its pacing target). The limiter stores a measured frame time in `+0x264`; nobody reads it in the 1.00 code. Further fixed 1/30 sites, among others in shard 0041 (`f_17f9b40`) and ≈ 25 call sites in shard 0047; 1/60 pairs in shards 0009/0010 [INFERENCE: fixed physics/clock step]. Consequence for DoD 4: native 60/120/144 fps require several patch sites; the safe way is a fixed 30 Hz simulation step plus frame interpolation at output.
    53. **Update 1.09 + DLC (evidence: header analysis, local extraction, `bbinstall validate`):** user PKGs are fake PKGs (DRM 0xF). EU update `CUSA03173` 01.09 (172 MB, category `gp`, 405 files incl. `eboot.bin` 93,825,704 B) was extracted **only locally** with a throwaway script outside the repo (EKPFS derived from the standard passcode, checked against the digests of the PKG entries – no key needed) and laid over the EU base via hardlinks (base checked unchanged). The DLC PKG (`SPEXPANSIONDLC03`, category `ac`) contains only the entitlement (no PFS); the content of The Old Hunters is in the GOTY data. Changed: `resolve_layout` accepts `Sc0/` without `Image0/`; `sceAppContentGetAddcontInfoList` reports installed add-on content from `BB_DLC_DIR` or `<app0>/../dlc` (entries of 24 B each, status 4 = installed). The installer still does not extract PKGs (user decision: local testing only). 1.09 recompile: `build/b109.cmd` → `build_109`.
    54. **1.09 run: two HLE bugs found (evidence: `BB_SAVE_LOG`, run 150 s with `build_109`):** (a) In the merged folder the update leaves an `Image0/sce_sys/` (keystone, about/) without `param.sfo`; `main.cpp` therefore did not mount `Sc0/` as `/app0/sce_sys` → title-ID fallback `CUSA00000` (different save folder) and default values for app parameters. Now the presence of `param.sfo` decides. (b) `sceAppContentAppParamGetInt`: the game queries IDs 0–4; ID 0 is the SKU flag (3 = full version), 1–4 are `USER_DEFINED_PARAM_1..4` – previously ID 0 returned the value of parameter 1 (13). Afterwards: `AppParamGetInt(0) = 3`, `(1) = 13` from `param.sfo`. Both HLE sites and SaveData now read `param.sfo` via `core/sfo` (moved to `bbimage`). Installer: game folder with `Sc0/` without `Image0/` is rejected with "Image0/ missing", Sc0-only DLC accepted (new CLI tests). Local hash manifest of the 1.09 folder (28,844 files, from only 1 dump, not committed) verified 28,844/28,844.

(b) Afterwards draws/dispatches read the DMA targets: buffers with pending GPU writes are – even under 64 KB – imported and ordered by a barrier instead of a CPU wait (unless the result still lies as a write-back in the ring); DISPATCH_INDIRECT with GPU-written arguments reads these via `vkCmdDispatchIndirect` (`BB_GPU_INDIRECT=0` = old; indirect *draws* still read synchronously, their start values go into user SGPRs). Full flushes 5745 → ≈ 270. (c) Guest submits that follow within 300 µs land in the same command buffer; the fence watcher submits it at the latest when that window elapses (`BB_COALESCE_US`, 0 = off): 41,700 → 16,400 submits. (d) 4 submit slots as default (slot wait 37 → 8–16 s). (e) Write watch: each 64 KB block is queried at most once per guest submit; own CPU writes (`gpu_watch`) force a re-query (`BB_WW_PER_CALL=1` = old; ponytail: writes by other guest threads during a submit are only seen by the next one). (f) Watcher takes the backend lock only via `try_lock` (profile before: `ZwWaitForAlertByThreadId` + `NtAlertThreadByThreadIdEx` ≈ 16 % of render-thread samples – [INFERENCE] lock handoffs, not measured individually); statistics timers only with `BB_GPU_LOG`/`BB_GPU_PROF` (effect not measured individually; default binary afterwards 53 ms in one run). Final status (default binary, single run without statistics): median 53 ms, screenshot 220 s clinic correct, mean 14.56 (`build/fin.png`).     59. **Frame outliers and texture freshness (Evidence: new `BB_FRAME_LOG=1` – one line per frame with draws, backend times, wait time for the backend lock, uploads, pipelines, full texture hashes – and `build/frame_spikes.py`, which compares frames > 40 ms with the rest; solo runs, window 120–180 s; `BB_GPU_LOG` counters; `BB_WW_VERIFY` now also for textures; solo A/B `build/ab_clinic.py`, 2 rounds; screenshots 150/200 s: clinic correct, mean 13.97–14.42):** Status at 58: 2.8 % of frames > 40 ms. (a) Every ≈ 16 s, three frames at 114–123 ms: in them a dispatch waited 81–95 ms for the backend lock, and in the same frame a synchronous flush (6–9 ms). [INFERENCE] The cause was budget eviction of the vertex cache (it forced a synchronous flush and destroyed the individual buffers under the lock; holder of the lock not measured) – supported only by the fact that the outliers disappeared with the changes. Vertex cache entries are now sub-ranges of fewer 256 MB buffers (VMA virtual blocks); replaced or evicted ranges are released only after their slot completes, so eviction needs no synchronous flush. Afterwards lock waits of 29–39 ms still occurred every ≈ 16 s; now an incremental sweep (512 hash buckets per flush) removes entries that have gone unused for 300 frames. Counters of a 190 s run: 348,593 removed entries, 0 budget evictions, 0 failed sub-allocations, 694 MB in use. (b) Every 64 frames, 564 full texture hashes in one frame (+10–20 ms): images checked first within the same frame stayed in lockstep; now each image has its own phase (≈ 11 full hashes per frame). Frames > 40 ms: 2.8 % → 0.9 % (sub-ranges + phase) → 0.2 % (sweep), longest frame 123 → 72 → 41 ms. (c) Texture check mode (`BB_WW_VERIFY`): every 64th "unchanged" verdict compares the full hash of the guest bytes with the uploaded state. At first, 16 of 50,908 checks were stale (13 within one epoch, 3 despite an identical sample), all the same 1024×1024 R8 texture (linear, `0x1117ee2100`); `BB_GPU_WATCH` (now also with length) showed no write access by the port in its range, so the game CPU writes it. Now an image whose bytes change after the first upload counts as dynamic and is checked via the write watch from then on (Linux: full hash): afterwards 36,582 checks, 0 stale, 2 dynamic textures; vertex `BB_WW_VERIFY` 2.27 million checks, 0 wrong. Validation run after (a): only the known `06479`/`09600`. **Solo A/B against the binary before (a)/(b):** median 33.0/33.0 ms, p95 36–37 / 37 ms, p99 38 / 43 ms, > 40 ms 0.2 % / 1.6–1.8 %, 30.00 / 29.90 fps, render thread 30.4–31.0 / 30.4–30.5 ms CPU per frame. (c) has no performance measurement: the A/B run afterwards ran while another program kept the GPU at 99 % utilization (both arms 42–47 ms median), and does not count. Open: only the clinic measured – scenes with streaming, UI atlases or cutscenes are not checked for performance or texture freshness; the first change to an image can still go unnoticed for up to 64 frames if it lies outside the sample.

    62. **Engine frame structure for DoD 4 (Evidence: static analysis of the recompiled binary, subagent; findings with shard:line, version 1.00):** Correction to earlier statements: `f_21b9830` is a spin lock, the main loop is `f_1e4a8b0` (`while (f_201a0d0())`, shard_0056:154524). One frame in `f_201a0d0`: window pump, limiter `f_2035ac0`, FrameEvent with fixed dt 1/30 (literal, shard_0061:19621), then `FD4TaskManager` (`f_f88f40`) with all task groups. The draw step has three phases: GX+0x600++ (frame number), recording of the scene into one of two GX lists (`f_21b5c20`), handover to the GXRenderThread (`f_219a0f0`; FD4 event = mutex + `pthread_cond`, list index switches). Render thread `f_21b7a30` plays the list (`f_219ac10` → executor `f_21b5e20`) and calls the flip job (→ `sceGnmSubmitAndFlipCommandBuffers`); one frame pipeline depth. Feedback from render to simulation found: only GX+0x608 (played-back frame number, controls reuse of dynamic buffers, `f_e486d0`/`f_e48750`); effect of further list callbacks open. The hidden 60 Hz mode (`Game.FlipMode` case 2) changes only the limiter's pacing target; the FrameEvent stays 1/30 – in the code this explains the "fast-forward" (item 44). Fixed time steps as literals: 1/30 63 times (46 FrameEvents), 1/60 47 times (32 FrameEvents with dt 1/60, shard_0047). Post effects are YEBIS 2 ([Silicon Studio 2015](https://www.siliconstudio.co.jp/en/news/pressreleases/2015/en1503yebis.html)), with camera velocity techniques and TAA history – [INFERENCE] the previous-frame camera lives in YEBIS; whether TAA is active is open. Verdict: rendering the same sim state multiple times is structurally possible, but not as a pure camera replay (constants are "baked" into the list, characters need object/skinning interpolation); 165 Hz requires a replay in the Vulkan backend. Open, among others: producer of the view/projection matrices, order of the task groups, side effects of `f_219ab60`. Next experiment E1 (`BB_REPLAY`): play the same list a second time after the flip and flip again; passed at ≈ 2× flips per sim frame, unchanged game speed, same image, no hang.

From 120–360 s, up to four other games from other measurements ran in parallel (frame times there 50–285 ms median, not meaningful); 360–660 s alone: median 33.3–33.5 ms, p99 38.5–41.5 ms, 0.2–1.8 % of frames > 40 ms, longest frame 156 ms (one outlier at 360–420 s, cause not determined). Memory: RSS after loading stable at 5.8–6.1 GB; private bytes rose in the quiet phase from 9,763 to 10,011 MB (≈ 0.8 MB/s, ≈ 3 GB per hour extrapolated) – cause open (guest allocations, image/pipeline caches or a leak in the port; not investigated).     64. **Render path shortened further (Evidence: subagent, paired runs `frame_breakdown.py` per step, solo A/B `ab_clinic.py` 2 rounds, gates; measurement clinic only):** kept: (a) texture memo per epoch (T# → image + view, valid until a new epoch, a new/replaced render/depth target, `tex_touch` or a new dirty range over the image) – first for simple textures (2293 hits per frame, views 1.68 → 1.19 ms), then also for render/depth targets and crops (4377 hits, views 0.69 → 0.54); (b) `get_shader` without heap allocation per call, fetch-code comparison directly in guest memory; (c) `views` as hash map, vertex-cache memo over 16 Ki slots, buffer path with one `mem_valid`/`maybe_dirty` each, LDS zeroing via `vkCmdFillBuffer` (pair: draw 27.15 → 25.02 ms, 25.3 → 26.5 fps); (d) descriptor update templates, VS and PS set in one allocation (pair: draw 23.29 → 22.86). Rolled back because no gain: shader memo (only 7 % hits, shader 4.25 → 5.54 ms) and skipping already-checked vertex entries. Corrections: the texture memo stamps the epoch read before `get_texture` (the present thread increments it without the backend lock); CPU-side PM4 write accesses (WRITE_DATA, DMA target, DUMP_CONST_RAM) call `tex_touch`; a failed vertex sub-allocation triggers eviction immediately; the retry after a pool flush allocated descriptor sets from the old, full pool – fixed. Sum of the pairs ≈ −10 % draw per frame; each pair measured once, steps under 5 % only indicate direction. Solo (2 rounds, status after (d) vs. before): both arms 33.0 ms median, p99 38 ms, 0.2–0.3 % > 40 ms, 30.00 fps, render thread 30.6–31.1 ms CPU per frame – at the game's 30 fps pacing no difference is measurable; the saved backend time does not appear as lower render-thread CPU ([INFERENCE] the thread waits or spins in step with the game's pacing; not investigated). Gates: `BB_WW_VERIFY` 3.04 million vertex checks 0 wrong, texture 19,490 checks 0 stale; screenshot mean 14.24; ctest 7/7; validation run with the templates (own run afterwards): clinic reached (screenshot 240 s, mean 14.27), only `06479` ×20 and `09600` ×2. According to the profile, the costs lie mainly in cache misses (freshly written guest data, map nodes): 10,528 buffer copies per frame at ≈ 280 B, 232 vertex uploads per frame, texture samples 9.5 MB per frame, NVIDIA driver ≈ 16 % of the render thread.
    65. **Unthrottled measurement mode `BB_UNCAP` (Evidence: subagent; runs uc3–uc5 without other games, `build/uncap_stats.py` compares per run the windows before and after switching; screenshot of the unthrottled phase):** `BB_UNCAP=<s>` stops the limiter from waiting from `<s>` seconds on: the limiter's first `gettimeofday` per frame (return address 0x2435ccc, only in 1.00) gets a time ≥ frame start + target; the offset then applies to all `gettimeofday` calls of the main thread (so the draw step's frame stamps stay consistent); other threads see real time (`src/hle/kernel.cpp`). Above 60 flips/s, `BB_FLIP_PACE=0` is additionally needed. The logic keeps computing 1/30 s per frame, so unthrottled the game runs faster (fast-forward); for measurements only. Without `BB_UNCAP` unchanged (throttled phase of the same runs: 30.0 fps, median 33 ms). **Result clinic:** unthrottled 39.9/40.5 fps, median 25 ms, p95 27–28 ms, p99 29 ms; render thread 22.4/22.6 ms CPU per frame (≈ 90 %), main thread 22.1/22.4 ms (≈ 90 %), GPU utilization 44 % – CPU-bound on both threads. With `BB_GPU_LOG` the statistics clock costs the render thread ≈ 6 ms per frame (28.3 instead of 22.4 ms, 34 instead of 40 fps): section times from `frame_breakdown.py` are inflated accordingly; measure fps without `BB_GPU_LOG`. **Correction to 58, 59, 64:** the "render thread 30–31 ms CPU per frame" cited there is the busiest thread reported by `ab_clinic.py` – when throttled, that is the main thread spinning in the limiter; the render thread was at ≈ 21 ms when throttled. `ab_clinic.py` now reports the two busiest threads. Visual effect: unthrottled screenshots show the clinic correctly (mean 14.6–15.0; brightness rises with game time already before switching). Consequence for 165 Hz (6.06 ms per displayed frame): both threads today need ≈ 22 ms each; [INFERENCE] the GPU needs ≈ 11 ms per frame (44 % of 25 ms), so a pure GPU replay would reach at most ≈ 90 fps – what is needed is therefore a backend replay without re-decoding, and less GPU time.
    66. **Memory leak in guest heap fixed (Evidence: subagent; 720 s soak runs with `VirtualQueryEx` evaluation per allocation base every 30 s, temporary allocation statistics; `BB_WW_VERIFY` run 300 s):** the growth from 63 (≈ 0.75–0.95 MB/s private bytes) consisted almost only of new 64 MB chunks of the HLE guest heap (`src/hle/guest_heap.cpp`, one every 60–90 s); the port's host heap (backend maps, `g_gpu_marks`, `g_page_epoch`) grew only ≈ 0.02 MB/s; guest mappings are created only during loading. The game calls ≈ 2000 `malloc`/`free` per second with a constant ≈ 16,600 live blocks (≈ 3 MB). Cause: in `free_plain`, `n->next = g_free[h->tag]` overwrote the field `h->tag` (same address), after which every block landed in the never-used list `g_free[0]` – frees were never reused. Now the size class is read first. Afterwards: private bytes in the static phase 9,685 → 9,684 MB over 420 s (≈ 0 MB/s), plateau ≈ 430 MB below the earlier final level; 720 s and 300 s without crash; `BB_WW_VERIFY` 3.74 million checks 0 wrong, textures 23,539 checks 0 stale. Regression test `tests/test_guest_heap.cpp` (ctest `guest_heap`: reuse of freed blocks per size class, 200,000 cycles without an additional chunk, aligned and large blocks) fails with the old code; ctest 8/8. Risk: frees are now really reused – a previously hidden use-after-free in guest or HLE code could become visible (nothing occurred in 17 min of clinic). Not fixed (not observed): aligned blocks over 1 MiB would be ignored when freed.
    67. **Experiment E1: guest replay of the GX list (Evidence: subagent, `BB_REPLAY=<s>` in `src/hle/thread.cpp` `gx_replay`, hook point at the mutex lock before the render thread's "done" event; 7 sets with 2–5 parallel runs each, incl. control runs, flip/frame log, list dumps, screenshots):** after the flip job the render thread plays the same list a second time (`f_2199d30`, executor `f_21b5e20`, GX+0x608 saved/restored) and flips again. Passed: exactly 2.0 flips per sim tick in all 11 replay runs (controls 1.0), GX+0x600 +1 per tick, 0 deviations from GX+0x608, no hang; the sim rate dropped only to the extent that the replay does work. Failed: the image – the second pass draws only 48 of ≈ 1520 draws (post/composite callback `0x25d6e20` again, scene geometry and HUD missing): the 60 draw packets free their chunk lists when played (`f_216bbc0`/`f_216bd70`), the HUD buffer is rebuilt per pass (`f_1f628c0`). With deferred release (`BB_REPLAY_KEEP=1`) the replay draws ≈ 1440 draws again, but crashes after 1–2 replay frames in the chunk allocator of a GX recording thread (switch kept only for reproduction). One of seven runs crashed after 57 s of replay in `pm4_execute` (not reproduced; [INFERENCE] the post callback's buffer switch shifted). Debug switches: `BB_REPLAY_DROP=<fn>`, `BB_REPLAY_SKIP=<fn>`, `BB_REPLAY_KEEP=1`. `call_guest` now takes a fourth argument (`rcx`). Consequence for DoD 4: the sim tick and flip path tolerate several flips per tick; but a replay in guest code consumes the list and costs the full PM4 build again – the way forward is a replay in the Vulkan backend (record the scene command buffer per sim frame, resubmit it with interpolated constants, re-run the post pass and HUD).
    68. **Feasibility probe backend replay: constants between sim frames (Evidence: subagent, `BB_CONST_DUMP=<ab s>[,<frames>[,<datei>]]` in `src/gpu/backend.cpp` `const_dump`, analysis `build/const_diff.py` with `--selftest`; one counted clinic run, 8 follow-up frames, motion via step-back keys 110–127 s):** per draw/dispatch, the shader, targets, user SGPRs, descriptor words and the bytes of constant/index buffers (≤ 64 KiB) and written buffers (≤ 4 KiB) are saved; pure vertex streams only as hash. Results: (a) 1518–1636 draws + 48 dispatches per frame; same position in the following frame only 67–77 %, key VS+PS+count+instances without RT 92–99.7 % (one pass rotates with period 3 over three color targets, a time-sliced pass has 15–115 more draws); constant addresses (ring) and user SGPRs almost always change – not a key. (b) 10,916 matched pairs: 9.2 % unchanged, 49 % only continuous floats (plus pointers, where applicable), ≈ 38 % with jumps/integers; changed dwords overall 81 % continuous (without the 4112-B post-FX buffers ≈ 89 %), discrete mainly in 416-B object buffers (pointers, flags) and 13-KB instance lists. (c) No compute skinning; palettes are VS buffers with 16-B stride (1223 references, 379 change per frame, 1.3 MB, 92 % continuous, mostly rigid 3×4 blocks); ≈ 102 vertex streams per frame with new CPU data (≈ 516 KB, particles/cloth [INFERENCE]). (d) Camera block 864 B (dword 0 far = 3000, 4–5 resolution, 8–19 view 3×4, 20–35 ViewProj, then Proj/InvProj …), 61–63 blocks per frame with 37–38 contents (main view, shadows, lights), 860 of ≈ 1550 draws; main view: 76–82 of 216 dwords change, all continuous; previous-frame matrices are not in there. Time uniform: global 176-B block, dword 36 +1/30 per frame. Game time advanced exactly 1/30 s per frame during the dump (≈ 100 ms per frame) (fixed sim step). Verdict: interpolation via dword mask (only continuous floats, otherwise a new frame) is feasible for camera, rigid transforms and palettes; always the newest list is played; dynamic streams and post-FX constants are not interpolated. Risks: ≈ 33 ms more latency, linear matrix interpolation slightly shrinks rotations, instance lists are re-sorted per frame, location of the previous-frame matrices for temporal effects unknown, one full GPU frame per displayed image (under 6 ms needed for 165 Hz). Next step: interpolate only the camera blocks. Side finding: see 70.
    69. **Profile main and render thread unthrottled (Evidence: subagent; 3 solo runs of the clinic with `BB_UNCAP`, built-in profiler and temporary stack sampler that pauses both threads at once, ≈ 6000–7000 samples per thread; 32.0/36.6/34.8 fps, the sampler costs ≈ one core):** main thread 23.9/24.3 ms CPU per frame, render thread 23.8/24.6 ms. **Critical path is the main thread, narrowly:** the render thread waits 2.1–2.5 ms per frame for the kick, the main thread ≤ 0.1 ms for the render thread – gains only on the main thread can bring at most these 2–2.5 ms; after that, the render thread's work is the limit. Main thread per frame: own work 21.9/23.0 ms (sim 11.4/11.9; self-executed GX jobs 8.9/9.3; serial draw step 1.6/1.7), polling for the end of the GX jobs 2.7/2.6 ms (GXWorker only 41 % active meanwhile), blocked on other guest threads 2.65/3.2 ms (cloth queue, WorldChrMan cond, hkbWorld/physics). Correction to `local://dod4_spike.md`: `f_21b9830` is not a pure spin lock; it waits for a GX job group and processes jobs meanwhile (`f_21baaa0`, wake-up call of the GXWorkers via `sceKernelSignalSema`). GX jobs ≈ 47 ms CPU per frame (main thread 9.3, five GXWorkers at ≈ 7.5 each, ≈ 4.4-fold parallel); only one HavokWorkerThread (11 % utilized; count from the game's cinfo, `f_4232f0`). Largest self times, main thread: `call_indirect` 8.3–8.6 % (63 % of it binary search over 179,038 `kFunctions` entries) + `find_import` 1.0–1.1 %, bytewise CRC64 `f_e48330` 2.1–2.6 %, rt helpers not generated inline (`f_sub`, `ld`, `xw` …) 4.5–4.7 %, waking via `sema_signal`/`ZwAlert` (wakes all five GXWorkers) ≈ 3.5 %. Render thread per frame: backend 19.2/19.9 ms (of which NVIDIA driver ≈ 5.5: BindPipeline 3.4 %, AllocateDescriptorSets 2.7, UpdateDescriptorSetWithTemplate 2.5, PipelineBarrier 2.5, BindDescriptorSets 1.8, BeginRendering 1.6), guest code 5.3/5.8 (YEBIS ≈ 1.4, Scaleform ≈ 2), kick waiting 2.1/2.5; inclusive `pm4_execute` 20.4 ms, `draw` 16.4, `write_descriptors` 8.3 (`get_texture` 2.8, `get_vertex_buffer` 2.5), `get_shader` 2.5; `hash_fast` 6.4 % (now only texture samples per epoch), write-watch polls 2.6–3.2 %. No GPU fence waits. Consequence: levers in order (a) `call_indirect` cache, (b) descriptor sets per draw (push descriptors or set cache), (c) checks per frame instead of per guest submit (only with `BB_WW_VERIFY`), (d) rt helpers inline + native CRC64, (e) targeted wake-up in `sema_signal`. Tool: `rungame.py` copies the matching map to `build\<name>.map` on `BB_PROFILE_SECS`, `prof.py` takes it (a relink during the run would otherwise produce wrong names).
    70. **VS buffer window clipped instance data and bone palettes – fixed (Evidence: subagent; probe run with GCN disassembly via `BB_SHADER_DIR`, screenshots 240 s, gates):** `write_descriptors` limited every non-scalar VS buffer with stride to the vertex window of the draw (highest index + 1, at least the instance count). That is correct only for streams that are read solely via the vertex ID (`vertex_use == 1`). Shown: instanced VS (`vs@10d76a0900`, `vs@10d7653300`, `vs@10d7d59400`) read `instance_id*13 + 3/5` and bones `(bone + v11)*3 + {0,1,2}` – e.g. V# 208 records (13 instances × 16), bound 68; V# 832, bound 201; skinning VS (`vs@10d8a99e00`, `vs@10d8a9b000`, 1 instance) palette `(bone + s0)*3 + k`, V# 252 records, bound 119 and 27 respectively. Everything beyond the window read 0 (later instances, high bone indices). Now only `vertex_use == 1` streams are clamped, all others are bound in full (64 MB cap remains); `cur_vtx_records = max(…, instances)` is dropped. Cost: 70–149 larger bindings per frame, 212–451 KB more per frame (largest 13 KB) – no fps measurement. Clinic image: no visible difference beyond run-to-run noise (pixels with deviation > 32: old/old 1023, new/new 5215, old/new 4439–6168; differences at the player figure and candle flicker); [INFERENCE] the affected draws lie outside the field of view or in other passes. Image pair `build/vtxclamp_before.png`/`vtxclamp_after.png`. Gates: build without warnings, ctest 8/8, `BB_WW_VERIFY` 1.93 million checks 0 wrong, textures 0 stale.
    71. **`call_indirect` via hash table (Evidence: subagent; profile `BB_UNCAP=260`, window 275–305 s, one pair each run sequentially and simultaneously against `bbgame_base_perf.exe`; `uncap_stats.py`; run 310 s with screenshot):** instead of binary search over 179,038 `kFunctions`, a read-only hash table (Fibonacci hash, linear probing, 2^19 slots, ≈ 8 MB, built during static init of `src/runtime/guest.cpp`); a hit jumps to the target via tail call; `lookup()` uses it too. PLT and synthetic import addresses in a second table (`find_import`: one access instead of `unordered_map`), HLE cache per import without static guards. Imports are counted only when `BB_HANG_SECS` or `BB_TRACE_HLE`/`BB_TRACE_ARG0`/`BB_TRACE_SLOW` is set (debug path `call_hle_debug`, unchanged). Result `call_indirect` + `find_import`: main thread 3.16/3.51 → 0.88 ms per frame, GXWorker 15.7 % → 3.5–4.0 % of their work time, render thread 0.80/1.18 → 0.23/0.28 ms. Unthrottled, sequentially: 31.6 → 34.5 fps, main thread 27.4 → 24.5 ms, render thread 28.3 → 26.3 ms CPU per frame – under load (other games, shard rebuild) and with changes by other agents in the new exe, so only the function counts can be cleanly attributed. Gates: `guest.cpp` without warnings (O2 flags, recompiled individually because the o2b log had been overwritten), ctest 8/8, 310 s without crash, screenshot mean 14.55. Risk: a call from the static init of another translation unit would see empty tables (currently none). Observed, not investigated: under load, phases with 1.7–10 fps from ≈ 110–120 s (also with the base exe); 208 million `gettimeofday` calls in the first 25 s after start.
    72. **Targeted wake-up in `sema_signal` – measured, no benefit, reverted (evidence: subagent; trace of the GX semaphore, paired profiles 277–315 s with two exes that differ only in `src/hle/thread.cpp`, 2–4 other games running alongside; uncapped, paired):** the GX semaphore (max 5) receives 12.5 signals per frame with 52 units in total (5 units: 61 %, 3: 15 %, 4: 12 %, 2: 7 %, 1: 5 %), all waits with need 1 and no timeout – "wakes all five workers" thus mostly matches the requirement. `notify_one` per unit (with waiter counter): `ZwAlert` 0.61 → 0.67 ms, blocked 9.36 → 9.90, polling in `f_21b9830` 4.27 → 3.90 ms, uncapped 25.9/25.8 flips/s – noise. `sema_signal` self time 0.01 ms (the 1.5 % from 69 were inclusive). Variants: notify under the lock lowers polling (4.28 → 1.97 ms), but the main thread then works off 2.6 ms more GX jobs itself (net 0); 50 µs spin before sleeping: 32.96 → 32.82 ms per frame (noise). Consequence: blocking and polling time of the main thread is real waiting for work (Havok workers, last GX jobs), not wake-up cost. Code reverted. Tooling: `rungame.py` now also reads `BB_PRIO` from the `KEY=VAL` arguments (previously paired runs ran at different priorities: spurious gain +20 %). Note: under foreign load the clinic is partly still on the loading screen at 150–205 s, so uncapped measurements start at `BB_UNCAP=260`.
    73. **DoD 4: backend replay `BB_INTERP=2`, steps 1+2 (evidence: subagent following design `local://replay_design.md`; clinic runs on a heavily loaded machine – 2–4 other games, CPU 37–100 % –, frame triples via `BB_SHOT_N`, `build/replay_check.py` with `--selftest`, validation run, own visual check of the triple):** per tick the Vulkan command buffers are kept as segments (`SIMULTANEOUS_USE`); ops that write guest memory (written buffers via import/write-back, GDS, `hook_gds_copy`, `hook_mem_copy`) go into their own "unsafe" segments and are skipped in the replay (the safe segment after them starts with a full barrier). Labels, EOP, CPU helper ops and flips are in no command buffer – the guest sees exactly one flip per tick. The tick slots (ring, sets, released vertex ranges) are held by a fence until the end of the replay; tick discarded on graveyard/import drop or slot wrap (39 of 4466 ticks, frame then doubled). `backend_replay_tick`: one submit, glue code copies the display RT, patches the 864-B camera blocks via `vkCmdCopyBuffer` (partner of the same class via dwords 0/4/5, next matrices dwords 8–35, cut at > 0.25 norm, lerp only for normal floats with jump ≤ 0.5·max(1,|a|,|b|)). Presenter (`present.cpp`): packets of n frames each (n = round(`BB_FPS`/30), `BB_INTERP_N`, `BB_INTERP_DELAY_MS`), phase-locked to due dates (free-running pacing gave only 55–57 distinct frames/s). Pad script can now hold the right stick (`rx=`/`ry=`, `src/hle/pad.cpp`). Off by default; without `BB_INTERP=2` unchanged. **Step 1 (`BB_INTERP_LERP=0`):** 60.0 presents/s and 60.0 distinct frames/s at 30.0 game frames/s; replay vs original of the same tick (20 triples) 0.0000 pixel share > 24, max. 2/255 on 0.13 % of pixels (reference orig k−1 vs k: 0.0011). **Step 2 (camera lerp):** 26 triples with right-stick turn, shift share mean/median 0.500 (std 0.002), 26/26 within 0.5 ± 0.1; per tick 38 camera blocks, 37–38 matched, 94–123 interpolated dwords per block. Triples `build/replay_cam_prev.png`/`replay_cam_half.png`/`replay_cam_cur.png` – visual check: the in-between frame visibly lies between both originals, no artifacts. **Cost:** GPU per replay median 12.0 ms, p95 13.1 ms (quiet camera), p95 24.9 ms during turn; render thread 0.50 ms per tick (p95 0.9). 2 × 300 s without crash. A/B vs `BB_INTERP=1` under foreign load not reliable (replay uses 8 instead of 6 slots). Gates: build without warnings, ctest 8/8; off: screenshot means 14.48/14.64, `BB_WW_VERIFY` 0 wrong (0.78 and 2.8 million checks), textures 0 stale; validation on: 06479/09600 as known, plus new `VUID-vkCmdCopyBufferToImage-imageSubresource-07968` ×10 – also occurs without replay with the right-stick script: the `get_texture` key ignores `LAST_ARRAY`, a cube-array T# with 36 layers hits an image with 6 layers (open). Open: temporal state (TAA history, adaptation run twice per tick; the display RT ends with the replay frame), figures and objects stay at 30 Hz (object blocks and palettes not interpolated), GPU budget allows at most n = 2–3 with ≈ 12 ms per replay, latency ≈ +1 tick, reading guest memory in the replay (3–8 imports per tick) no finding, solo A/B missing.
    74. **Push descriptors (evidence: subagent; A/B with the same exe `BB_PUSH_DESC=0` vs default, profile window 165–195 s, `uncap_stats.py`, almost always other games running alongside; gates with push and with fallback):** `VK_KHR_push_descriptor` is enabled when available (`vk_context.cpp`, `VkCtx::max_push`); VS and PS share set 0 (`GcnEnv::binding_base`, binding `2*i + base`, VS/CS even, PS odd). Per VS/PS pair resp. CS one set layout, pipeline layout and template (`desc_layout`); if the resource count fits `maxPushDescriptors`, it is pushed via template, otherwise pool path (2.1–2.3 % of draws/dispatches). `prepare_descriptors` (may flush) fills `wd_info`, `bind_descriptors` pushes or binds. Render thread per frame (profile): driver 7.63 → 5.12/6.16 ms, descriptor path 2.54 → 1.90/2.12 ms, busy 36.5 → 27.8/30.8 ms; per draw push 893 ns instead of alloc 364 + update 610 + bind ≈ 490 ns. Without profiler uncapped 23.2 → 26.5 fps, render thread 31.8 → 30.3 ms CPU – under foreign load, rough only. A set cache by content would gain nothing (ring offsets change per draw); `write_descriptors` itself (≈ 2 ms per frame) remains the next lever. Gates: build without warnings, ctest 8/8, `BB_WW_VERIFY` 2.28 million checks 0 wrong, textures 0 stale, validation with push and with `BB_PUSH_DESC=0` only 06479/09600, screenshots 14.56–14.82. [INFERENCE] `maxPushDescriptors` on NVIDIA 32 (not logged); on RADV the fallback share may differ.
    75. **Texture identity with layer count (evidence: subagent; validation run with right-stick script `50:cross,67:cross,82:cross,90:cross,96:cross,101:rx=200`, ≈ 300 s; temporary T# logger):** `get_texture` keyed by DEPTH instead of layer count – a cube array with 36 layers hit an existing 6-layer image at the same address and uploaded over it (`VUID-vkCmdCopyBufferToImage-imageSubresource-07968` ×10). Now layers = max(DEPTH, LAST_ARRAY+1), rounded up to multiples of 6 for cubes, in the key. Afterwards `VkImageViewCreateInfo-viewType-02960` occurred (CUBE view with 36 layers, the shader declares a single cube): CUBE views now have exactly 6 layers starting at the cube that contains BASE_ARRAY; ponytail: cube arrays are sampled as a single cube. Afterwards validation only 06479/09600, `BB_WW_VERIFY` 3.57 million checks 0 wrong, textures 19,163 checks 0 stale, ctest 8/8, screenshot reviewed. T# fields checked: pitch never differs for the same key; BASE_ARRAY only view range. Found along the way: no mip support (every image `mipLevels = 1`; three mip chains written via `STORE` `0x105b0c0100`, `0x105b169d00`, `0x105b170900` wrote into level 0) – fixed in 79.
    76. **GPU time per frame broken down (evidence: subagent; new `BB_GPU_TS=<N>` in `src/gpu/backend.cpp` – timestamps around each rendering instance, each dispatch, each barrier, copy, clear and the upload buffer, read at normal slot completion, output every N frames; evaluation `build/gpu_ts.py`, discards blocks with foreign GPU load; three clinic runs, 480–660 frames; with `BB_GPU_TS` `BB_INTERP=2` is off):** GPU busy 13.0–13.6 ms per frame (37–44 % of the frame time 30.6–35.7 ms) – the GPU is not the choke point. Per frame: passes 5.3–5.6 ms, ≈ 780 full barriers 3.0–3.7 ms, idle gap after barriers 1.2–1.3 ms ([INFERENCE] largely overhead of the ≈ 1570 timestamps; without it ≈ 11.7 ms), copies 0.95–1.33, compute 0.61, upload 0.07 ms. Largest pass: G-buffer 1920×1088, 6 × RGBA8 + depth/stencil, `ps@10d76aac00`, ≈ 505 draws, 1.93–2.06 ms; then 66 single-draw passes RGBA16F depending on one tessellation dispatch (`vs@10d95d5b00` as CS), 0.67 + 0.27 ms. Cost centers from our translation: 274 duplicate barriers with no work between them (0.84 ms), 261 full barriers at pass changes (1.36 ms), 31–103 crop copies of padded RTs with two barriers each (copy 0.6–1.2 ms + 0.47 ms), tessellation per patch draw: dispatch + barrier + new rendering instance, barrier after each of the 116 dispatches (0.34 ms). Shader runtime itself is no lever. Implication: the replay (73) costs ≈ 12 ms GPU per frame; for 165 Hz it must come down to ≈ 5 ms – barriers and copies are the lever. Gates: build without warnings, ctest 8/8, `BB_WW_VERIFY` 1.43 million checks 0 wrong/0 stale.
    77. **DoD 4: replay steps 5 (objects, palettes) and 3 (intermediate state) (evidence: subagent; 40 triples with straight running via pad script `lx=`/`ly=`, `build/replay_check.py` measures the figure relative to the camera, `build/carry_check.py` compares original frames frame-exactly across runs (`BB_SHOT_CLOCK=pad`); cost in the quiet window; validation; own visual check of the triple):** per draw, small constant blocks (≤ 4 KiB, without camera block) are captured, and for draws with one instance the bone palettes (in replay mode palettes run through the ring instead of the vertex cache so they can be patched). Matching across ticks: VS+PS+count+instances without RT, position in the key first, otherwise nearest constants; unchanged draws skipped via hash; lerp with the same continuity rule, cut at distance > 0.25. `BB_INTERP_OBJ=0` disables only the objects. Per tick ≈ 1470 draws, 935 with changed constants, 929 matched, 221 cuts, 31,000 interpolated dwords, 54 palettes, 0.6 MB staging. **Figure:** ratio median 0.502 (24/35 triples within 0.5 ± 0.1; outliers at turn/animation phases), without object lerp 0.88 (2/26); at most 0.57 % new pixels in the replay, no torn meshes (6 triples inspected). Triples `build/replay_char_prev.png`/`_half.png`/`_cur.png` – own visual check: figure in the in-between frame lies between both positions. **Intermediate state (`BB_INTERP_RESTORE`, default on):** a frame that is first read and then written in the tick gets a copy of the pre-tick state before the write, and a copy of the end state at the end of the tick; before each replay the pre-tick state is restored, after the last one the end state (15 + 21 copies per tick, **738 MB VRAM**). Original frames during camera turn, share of pixels > 24 vs OFF: with snapshot 0.25 %/0.93 %, without 0.63 %/0.74 %, OFF vs OFF 0.95 % – at noise level, the benefit is hardly measurable in this scene. **Cost:** GPU per replay 11.6 ms (p95 12.3; copies not measured separately), `replay_tick` 2.85 ms CPU (previously 0.5), render thread 21.8 → 24.7 ms CPU per frame (`ab_clinic.py`, both 30.00 fps). Gates: build without warnings, ctest 8/8, OFF unchanged (screenshot 14.50, `BB_WW_VERIFY` 1.47 million 0 wrong, textures 0 stale); validation found two errors in the snapshot logic (barrier inside a running rendering instance, copy of never-written images) – fixed, afterwards only 06479/09600. Endurance run: final binary 300 s without crash, but under 5 other games only 118 clinic ticks; previous version 230 s/3300 ticks error-free. Still at 30 Hz: draws with multiple instances (instance lists re-sorted per frame), palettes ≥ 64 KiB or with pending GPU writes, particles and cloth (CPU streams). `BB_GPU_TS` and replay are mutually exclusive. Side finding in all three images (also the originals): vertical stripes in a ceiling band (≈ y 150–180 at 1568 px width) – render error independent of the replay, cause open.
    78. **Fewer barriers (evidence: subagent; solo in quiet window, throttled, `BB_GPU_TS=60`, 720 clinic frames each, A/B in the same exe via `BB_BARRIER_OPT=0`; sync validation `VK_KHRONOS_VALIDATION_VALIDATE_SYNC=1` before/after; screenshots with right-stick script; `BB_INTERP=2` with `replay_check.py`):** (1) `barrier()` returns if nothing has been recorded since the last barrier (`rcb()` for all copy/clear/dispatch/fill commands, draws set it themselves; a new command buffer or replay segment always starts with a real barrier); (2) pass changes only with a barrier on a real dependency (non-draw work since then, UAV draws, WAR/WAW via `pass_read`, RAW via `pass_written`); (5) after dispatch only compute-shader write as source; (3, minor) no barrier after crop copies, consecutive crops share one barrier before them. Result per frame: barriers 772 → 451, barrier time 2.84 → 1.93 ms, passes 5.42 → 5.80 ms ([INFERENCE] the pipeline drain now counts toward the pass), GPU busy 12.53 → 12.08 ms (−3.6 %); frame time unchanged 33.3 ms. Gates: sync validation before and after only the known `SYNC-HAZARD-WRITE-AFTER-READ` on the swapchain image after `vkAcquireNextImageKHR` (presenter), 0 new hazards; screenshots within noise; `BB_WW_VERIFY` 0/0; replay vs original 0.0000 (4 triples, camera still); build without warnings, ctest 8/8. Consequence: for ≈ 5 ms per replay this falls far short – G-buffer ≈ 2.0 ms, 66 tessellation passes with compute ≈ 1.2 ms, crops ≈ 1.4 ms remain; [INFERENCE] at ≈ 12 ms per frame, at most ≈ 1.75 replays fit into each 33-ms tick alongside the original (≈ 80 frames/s); 165 Hz additionally needs a cheaper in-between-frame path. Open: the presenter hazard (acquire semaphore does not cover the transition).
    79. **Mip chains (evidence: subagent; paired runs with the same exe copy before/after, right-stick script, other games running alongside; ctest `check_mips`; validation; `BB_WW_VERIFY`):** `mip_chain()` (`src/gpu/tiling.h`) computes the guest layout per level according to the addrlib rules: levels after level 0, each with all slices; pitch/height halved per level, with POW2_PAD (word 3, bit 25) to powers of two; linear-aligned rows 64 B, slices 256 B; 1D 8×8, 256-B slices; 2D in whole macro tiles, smaller levels as 1D (micro order of the mode); thick and BC covered; ponytail: no PRT mip tail. Images with BASE_LEVEL or LAST_LEVEL > 0 get the full chain (`bit_width(max(w,h))` levels; storage writes to level n and later sampling hit the same image); upload per level in one `vkCmdCopyBufferToImage`; if LAST_LEVEL grows later, only new levels are loaded, as long as the old bytes are unchanged (GPU-generated mips stay). Sampled/full hash, write watch and `tex_memo` cover the whole read chain. Views: BASE_LEVEL..LAST_LEVEL, storage views one level, level range in the view key. `get_sampler`: MIN_LOD/MAX_LOD in the key (previously variants shared one sampler); MIP_FILTER 1 = NEAREST, 2 = LINEAR, 0 = base level only (previously every value ≠ 0 was LINEAR). `twin()`/`copy_image()` (replay) copy all levels. In the clinic there is no render target within a mip chain (temporary logger) – RT writes to levels not built. Result: 804–830 of 1050–1236 texture images with multiple levels; upload over the run 355 → 488 MB (+37 %); image-to-image difference with still camera (`BB_SHOT_N=4`) 0.19 → 0.07 (−62 % flicker, mean unchanged 8.95); distant section practically the same (clinic has little distant view). GPU time under foreign load contradictory – A/B in the quiet window missing. Gates: build without warnings, ctest 8/8 incl. `check_mips`, validation only 06479/09600, `BB_WW_VERIFY` 1.16 million 0 wrong, textures 6,506 checks 0 stale, screenshot normal. Not done: anisotropic filtering (S# MAX_ANISO, needs `samplerAnisotropy`), mips for 3D textures, PRT mip tail.
    80. **Sample padded RTs directly, LS barriers, presenter hazard (evidence: subagent; solo in quiet window, throttled, `BB_GPU_TS=60`, 720 frames each, one exe with switches; sync validation; screenshots with tessellated geometry and right-stick script; `BB_INTERP=2`):** (1) For 2D/2D-array samples the shader scales the coordinates by (T# size at runtime)/(size of the bound image) and clamps to the center of the last T# texel; gradients of `image_sample_d` likewise (bit-exactly unchanged when sizes match, via select); `Resource::sampler` stores the sampler per image. The backend binds a padded target directly only with clamp-to-edge in the affected axes (or pure load, or exactly one matching sampler) and 2D without layers, otherwise the copy crop remains; switch `BB_PAD_DIRECT=0`. Copies 31 → 11 per frame, 0.95 → 0.38 ms. Side effect: larger depth buffers (2048×1152 for 1920×1080 T#), which were already sampled directly before, are now scaled ([INFERENCE] correction; no difference in the image). (2) Tessellation: no batching (would need deferred recording, collides with the replay segments); LDS up to 256 KB zeroed on the CPU, LS pass without barrier before it when only draws have run since and it samples none of them: barriers 451 → 385 per frame. (3) Presenter: transition UNDEFINED → COLOR_ATTACHMENT of the swapchain image with source stage COLOR_ATTACHMENT_OUTPUT (the acquire semaphore's stage) instead of TOP_OF_PIPE – sync validation afterwards 0 hazards (previously > 2300 × the same case). Measurement per frame (everything off via switches → barriers+LS → + direct sampling): GPU 12.61 → 12.17 → 12.00 ms, barriers 2.71 (772) → 1.60 (385) → 1.64 (369), passes 5.59 → 6.09 → 6.37, copies 0.91 → 0.95 → 0.38 ms; part of the saving reappears in the passes. Gates: build without warnings, sync validation 0 hazards (otherwise only 06479/09600), `BB_WW_VERIFY` 1.68 million 0 wrong/0 stale, screenshots within noise, replay vs original 0.0000–0.0001; ctest not repeated for this status.
    81. **Anisotropic filtering (evidence: subagent; solo slot, one exe, 1800 frames each `BB_GPU_TS`; temporary S# logger; validation with `BB_ANISO=16`):** `get_sampler` evaluates MAX_ANISO_RATIO (S# word 0, bits 11:9: 1/2/4/8/16) like GCN only with anisotropic filter (filter value 2 = point, 3 = linear), capped by `maxSamplerAnisotropy` (`VkCtx::aniso`), in the sampler key. The game requests 4× for its aniso-linear samplers; bilinear samplers with the ratio field set correctly get no aniso. `BB_ANISO=<1..16>` overrides the value for all samplers with linear minification. GPU per frame: off 11.06 ms, game value 11.24, 16× 11.17 (within noise); image in the dark clinic hardly different (tablecloth at 16× somewhat more detailed). 3D textures of the clinic: two 16³ without mips – 3D mips not needed. Gates: build without warnings, ctest 8/8, validation only 06479/09600, `BB_WW_VERIFY` 3.29 million 0 wrong, textures 17,604 checks 0 stale.
    82. **Giant functions ran unoptimized; rt helpers inlined; native CRC64 (evidence: subagent; paired profiles 167–197 s against `bbgame_base_perf.exe` and against the status before inlining; `uncap_stats.py`; 200,000 random CRC cases against the recompiled output; run 330 s):** MSVC compiled 76 generated giant functions (incl. draw-command emitter `f_26bc830`, Havok `f_b0ded0`/`f_b07f50`, Lua VM `f_2ba89a0`) without optimization (C4883, invisible at /W1) – therefore almost all calls to non-inlined helpers were generated. Now: CMake option `BB_OPTIMIZE_HUGE_FUNCTIONS` (default on) sets `/d2OptimizeHugeFunctions /w14883`; functions with > 2000 instructions and no jump/jump target get `#pragma optimize("", off)` (7 functions with no profiling sample; otherwise `f_392420` alone takes > 70 min to compile). `BB_INLINE` (`__forceinline`/`always_inline`) for 67 helpers in `guest.h`, parity via `__popcnt16`/`__builtin_parity`; `crc64_idiom` in the emitter recognizes `f_e48330` by its 46 bytes and calls `crc64_bytes` (0 deviations in registers/flags/memory in 200,000 cases, planted error → 701 detected; 2.2 instead of 6.2 ns/byte). Per frame: helpers main thread 1.48 → 0, GXWorker 2.16 → 0 ms; `f_e48330` 0.79 → 0.24 (main) / 1.87 → 0.35 (worker); giant functions 1.32 → 0.48 / 2.03 → 0.85. Uncapped against the status before inlining: main thread 22.8 → 20.4 ms (−11 %), GXWorker 6.7 → 5.8 ms each, render thread unchanged 24.5 – fps equal (37.1/37.2), the render thread is the limit. Inlining without the flag alone was a regression (giant functions 1.3 → 4.6 ms). Cost: full shard rebuild ≈ 208 instead of ≈ 80 CPU minutes (largest shard 24 min, cl.exe up to 5.6 GB) – `o2b.cmd` now builds with `-j 4`, lock applies for up to 3 h. Gates: ctest 8/8, build without warnings (also no C4883), screenshot 15.1, 330 s without crash. Next step (estimated, not measured): dead-flag elimination in the emitter – 24–38 % of flag computations are dead in the block, ≈ 3–6 % guest CPU.
        Correction: `o2b.cmd` builds again with `-j 2` (user's instruction, he works on the PC in parallel); the full rebuild with `/d2OptimizeHugeFunctions` thus takes ≈ 1.5–2 h, the lock applies for up to 3 h. Game runs: at the user's request `rungame.py` allows at most two games at the same time (slot directories `build\run_slots\0..1` with the owner's PID, orphaned slots are taken over; further runs wait).

Budget: (original + replays) ≤ 85 % of a tick, −1 frame when the render thread waits > 2 ms, +1 after 30 ticks without waiting, at budget 1 a forced replay every 30 ticks; `BB_INTERP_N=<n>` fixed. `BB_FPS=<n>` (MAILBOX, own pacing), `refresh` (FIFO, pacing from measured intervals), `unlimited`. Measurement: 60/120/165 Hz → 60.0/120.0/165.0 presents/s, 53.6/57.2/60.0 distinct frames/s, 30.0 game frames/s, interval p99 17.0/8.8/9.9 ms, 2 frames per tick, GPU per replay 10.6–10.7 ms, render thread 24.3/24.9/26.1 ms CPU per frame (without replay earlier 21.8); `refresh` (165 Hz): 152.1 presents/s, p95 14.1 ms – the presenter queues on the same queue behind the replays and misses individual vsyncs. A later short run at 165 Hz showed 31–60 distinct frames/s (cause open). `replay_tick` 2.3 → 2.0 ms CPU (matching via the first 64 dwords, table with generation counter instead of hash maps); intermediate state now only for images first read in ≥ 2 ticks within distance ≤ 16, released after 300 unused ticks: 738 → 164 MB. Gates: build without warnings, ctest 8/8, off: screenshot 14.81, `BB_WW_VERIFY` 1.44 million 0 wrong, textures 0 stale; validation at 165 Hz (before the last budget adjustment) only 06479/09600. Consequence: at ≈ 11 ms per replay it stays at ≈ 2 distinct frames per tick (≈ 60/s) – the user's decision: hybrid of real replays and depth reprojection for the remaining in-between frames.

Where the write watch does not apply (Linux, unwatched memory, `BB_TEX_WW=0`), the old path remains (not compiled under Linux, review only). `tex_memo` entries survive the epoch change if the whole image area is unwritten (own `BB_WW_VERIFY` sample); `tex_touch` counts per image (`touched`) instead of globally `tex_gen`. `write_watch.cpp`: poll state in a table (16 MiB leaves, page epoch in the 64 KiB chunk) instead of `unordered_map`; GPU writes (`ww_mark`) set the same page epochs (the unboundedly growing `g_gpu_marks` map is dropped; now exact per page); consecutive due 1 MiB blocks in one call. Result per frame: `draw()` 9.66 → 8.7 ms (−10 %), `write_descriptors` 5.4 → 4.6, `tex_ms` 1.60 → 1.16, `vtx_ms` 1.52 → 1.22; GetWriteWatch 0.41 → 0.88 ms (110 → 231 calls, 113 → 310 MB polled); render-thread shares `hash_fast` 5.5 → 0.8 %, `NtGetWriteWatch` 2.5 → 5.8 % (net ≈ −3.8 percentage points). Unthrottled frame time 18.1 → 17.7 ms – now the main thread is the limit (for comparison, 65: 25 ms). Discarded (measured): polling cold chunks only once per frame (−6 % volume, costs exactness), large textures back to sampling, polling only exact query ranges (−40 % data, 2.4× calls, same time). "replay vs. original" 0.030/0.022.
